# 1.9 sequel verification record

Milestone moved to 1.9 on 5 October 2026. This filename and dated entries
retain their original references for evidence continuity. 1.8 is Mac polish.

## Scope and source

The 1.9 exit gate covers all 120 Lemmings 2 levels and all 90 Lemmings 3
levels. It also requires continuous campaigns, source-engine rule comparisons,
endings, media, saved-run recovery and result identity. Keep both games labelled
Preview until the full gate passes.

This check started on 29 September 2026 from `release/1.7.0` at
`2e15cc14f87328809ab31e80255ab7e159b62780`. The Mac ran macOS 26.5.2
with Xcode 26.6. The working tree had four unrelated modified files before
this check. They were left untouched. This is a working-tree check, not a
frozen release candidate.

## Strict route checks

| Gate | Result | Remaining proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L2 fixture manifest | Passed: 75 routes and 27 carry-over variants have matching hashes. | None for the recorded fixtures. |
| L2 strict replay | Failed its completion condition after replaying 75 recorded routes. | 45 missing routes and 11 incomplete tribe chains. Only Cavelems chains through all ten levels. |
| L3 fixture manifest | Passed: the campaign fixture hashes and coverage manifest match. | None for the recorded fixtures. |
| L3 strict replay | Failed its completion condition after replaying 41 recorded routes. | 49 levels have no winning fixture. |

The L2 command was `zsh Scripts/verify-lemmings2-completion.sh --require-all`.
The L3 command was `zsh Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh --require-all`, linked
to the library built by the L2 gate from this source tree. Both used the Xcode
26.6 compiler and project-local module caches. The L3 gate stops at its strict
coverage failure, so its later negative-gate test did not run in this pass.

These failures report missing proof. They do not establish that an uncovered
level is unsolvable or that a specific rule is wrong.

On 30 September, the isolated `codex/l2-l3-verification` checkout at
`efdf6ca` ran both non-strict repository gates from its own compiled source.
The L2 gate passed all 73 recorded routes, 27 carry-over variants and its
negative checks for refused inputs, altered witnesses, duplicate starts and
orphan carry-over records. The L3 gate passed all 41 recorded routes and its
negative checks for a missing fixture, changed population, level hash, level
number or outcome, rejected input and out-of-order input. These checks confirm
that the recorded evidence is replayable and the selected invalid records fail
closed. They do not close the missing 47 L2 and 49 L3 route gaps.

The existing L3 solver searched uncovered Classic level 006 for 60 seconds.
Five widening rounds reached beam width 2048 and expanded 799,278 states.
It found no winning route and promoted no fixture. This bounded attempt is a
search result, not a level failure or a rule-parity result.

The L2 solver initially failed to compile under Xcode 26.6 because its exit
distance expression exceeded the compiler's type-check limit. The expression
was split into explicit centre and distance steps without changing its score.
The repository solver script then ran a 60-second search for `classic-06` with
the one survivor passed by the verified Classic chain. It reached beam width
2048 and expanded 293,620 states, but found no winning route. It promoted no
fixture. This is also a bounded search result, not a level failure.
The focused L2 solver suite passed, including scoring order, fixture promotion
rules and recovery of a damaged Classic level 01 seed to 60/60 saved.
The separate single-lemming reference search examined L2 Classic level 06
from the same one-survivor start. It generated candidates through depth two
and found no win within its search bounds. No fixture was changed.
An upstream 120-second search for a better Classic level 02 route used the
recorded 1/60 route as its seed. It expanded 93,317 states and found a 1/60
win only. It did not improve the carry-over population or change a fixture.

From `96d1d8492c45570eed0760f9e05407c3d55ef99c`, a bounded L2 Circus
level 04 search used the two survivors from the recorded Circus level 03
chain. With a 90-second limit and initial beam width 128, it widened to 256,
expanded 282,409 states and found no win. It wrote only an ignored partial
search record; no replay candidate or fixture was promoted. Circus level 04
and its continuous chain remain open.

On 30 September, a second bounded L3 search covered uncovered Classic levels
007, 008, 009 and 011. Each received 45 seconds with an initial beam of 128.
No winning route was found or promoted. The runs expanded 670,541, 928,994,
854,785 and 478,646 states respectively. A further pass with the solver's
fewer-inputs-first ranking and 90 seconds per level found no winning route.
It expanded 1,199,582, 1,706,242, 1,496,581 and 981,146 states. No fixture
was promoted. These searches do not establish that the levels are broken.

A further isolated search from `626f701` covered uncovered L3 Classic
levels 012, 014 and 017 on 30 September. Each used a 60-second budget and
initial beam width 128, with promotion enabled only for a route that passed
two exact-level replays. All three were unsolved within their budgets. They
expanded 898,933, 795,118 and 990,944 states respectively. No candidate
fixture was written or promoted.

An experimental lead-lemming distance ranking was tested from `dd7c67b` on
uncovered L3 levels 106, 205 and 206, with 45 seconds and initial beam 128
per level. It found no winning route. The searches expanded 424,918, 716,296
and 180,960 states respectively. The optional ranking was removed after the
run; no solver or fixture change was retained.

From `549f1fac9189166ae9c86175ba47f572237a15d3`, a temporary solver
copy capped routes at 64 accepted inputs and used the fewer-inputs-first
ranking on previously untested L3 Classic levels 024 and 025. Each search had
60 seconds and an initial beam width of 128. Level 024 expanded 571,411 states
and reached beam width 1024; level 025 expanded 827,206 and reached width 512.
Neither found a win or wrote a replay candidate. The input cap remains an
experiment in ignored build files; no solver or fixture change was promoted.
Further searches need stronger route guidance or resolved rule parity, rather
than repeating these bounds.

## Original walkthrough cross-check

From `83b5d05`, the bundled L3 Shadow level 106 was rendered and its native
object lists inspected. Its permanent object file contains no shimmier tool
identifier `5003`. A [PC Chronicles walkthrough](https://www.cheatbook.de/wfiles/lemmingschronicles.htm)
uses a shimmier pickup in its Shadow level 6 route. That route is unsuitable
as a seed for this bundled level without further source-version comparison.
No fixture was added. The local `LEVEL106.DAT` and `PERM106.OBS` SHA-256
values were `9a16a9e94700b0e827b297411f6bdffe994abe1d6c3c96a810a3ab83f44bea42`
and `508a0436b786277d997481508b75bc8a6e45b91c5294d9e8b4de92abff9c61ea`.

The same walkthrough describes Classic level 6 using bricks and a spade. The
bundled level 006 has those tools, including brick boxes beyond the
starting area, so it is a better guide candidate. Its prose implies more than six uses from the first
brick box, while the current preview gives each brick box six. This is a
source-engine quantity question, not proof that the preview is wrong. Resolve
it against the original game before changing tool rules or promoting a guided
route.

A [contemporary Amiga gameplay document](https://www.lemonamiga.com/doc/all-new-world-of-lemmings/59)
shows eight uses for a brick pickup. It does not establish the DOS CD rule.
From `e2894c1`, a temporary eight-use brick and spade setting was used only
for one bounded Classic level 006 search. With a 90-second solver budget and
initial beam 128, it expanded 684,253 states and found no win. The six-use
source was restored after the run; no candidate or fixture was retained.
The quantity question remains open.

From `38768f8c0286f178cccc985ec0ff76a8d363f2b5`, a guided Classic
level 006 search turned the first lemming left at tick 56. The native runtime
accepted the input and the lemming collected the first brick pickup. With the
unchanged six-use rules, beam width 128, maximum depth 220 and a 120-second
limit, the standard ranking expanded 119,414 states in 11 seconds and found
no win. The fewer-inputs-first ranking expanded 195,893 states in 17 seconds
and also found no win. Both searches exhausted their reachable beam before
the time limit. No replay candidate or fixture was written. This checks one
early turn from the walkthrough; it does not resolve the quantity or rule
parity question.

From `5555b302a91b254da936733ba7796a7d2bbca721`, the repository L3 solver
searched previously untested Classic levels 020, 022 and 023 with a 45-second
limit and initial beam width 128 per level. Three widening rounds reached
beam width 512 in each run. The searches expanded 954,407, 728,409 and
853,672 states respectively. None found a winning route, wrote a candidate
or changed a fixture. These bounded failures leave all three route gaps open.

## Source-level campaign and recovery checks

On 30 September, the isolated checkout at `66eac7d` passed
`zsh Scripts/run-lemmings2-runtime-tests.sh` and
`zsh Scripts/run-lemmings3-runtime-tests.sh` with the supplied local game data.
The Mac ran macOS 26.5.2 and Apple Swift 6.3.3. The Command Line Tools were
the active developer directory; installed Xcode 26.6 reports the same Swift
version.

The L2 suite loaded and advanced all 120 levels through native physics,
replayed 73 distinct recorded completions and selected Cavelems and Circus
carry-over routes. It passed twelve-tribe progress, medal thresholds, save
round trips, invalid-save rejection and ark eligibility checks. The
all-gold ending state in this suite is constructed campaign data, not a
played 120-level campaign or an observed ark ending.

The L3 suite checked the 30-level inventory for each tribe, selected Classic,
Shadow and Egyptian winning runs, survivor and reserve carry, isolated tribe
progress, save round trips, impossible-population rejection, skips and nine
trap types. It did not complete all 90 levels, compare a DOS result or test a
packaged app session. These source checks narrow the campaign-state risk but
do not close the 1.8 exit condition.

## Other open conditions

- Compare L2 movement and outcomes with original-game traces across the full
  campaign. Existing focused rule tests and one walking sample do not prove
  full source-engine parity.
- Resolve L3's provisional tool, movement, trap, creature, collision and
  animation rules against original-game evidence. No original-game level-play
  or frame-timing comparison is recorded.
- Verify continuous L2 survivor carry-over through all twelve tribes, plus
  continuous L3 progression and both games' endings.
- Complete L3 environmental sound, original movie soundtracks and automatic
  story transitions. The current movie gallery has silent, manual playback.
- Verify packaged app journeys, saved-run recovery, replay identity and result
  records on the candidate that will carry the Complete label.

DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 was installed on 30 September. The repository's
reference launcher booted both original games into VGA graphics mode with
display access outside the sandbox. That initial boot did not record a matched
level run, input sequence or final result. Source disassembly and native
replays remain useful, but cannot replace an original-game result comparison.
Physical input, audio and display checks also need a frozen candidate and a
recorded hardware matrix.

On 30 September, from `ce6572ab279e0616ac2f631ac8109d2709c2ddc5`,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 played the supplied L3 CD executable through a no-input
Shadow level 1 run. The original showed 20 lemmings on its level-selection
screen, then ended with **Out of time** and **You saved no lemmings this
time**. A native no-input run on bundled `LEVEL101.DAT` reached tick 6900,
released 10, saved 0, lost 10 and kept 10 in reserve. The two engines agree
on this one visible saved-count result. The DOS result did not expose its
internal lost or reserve counts, and no frame timing or tool action was
matched. The level data SHA-256 was
`ee624776ad7ba103a00ff1f47e555c688c12a1301ed16c7c4ab0c747ab9919b1`.
The ignored local capture
`.build/sequel-reference/l3.ecMFwE/captures/shadow-101-result.png` has SHA-256
`879ed87bbec0f3217f7b099f3da604c3eb2010afc78756903a834b5e396837fb`.
This closes one passive result comparison only; the broader original-engine
condition remains open.

From `bb6b9db83bc6da69376df79edf0e7e10075f2b2f` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 ran the supplied L3 CD game from the opening map room
through Classic Island level 1 without a tool action. The level selector showed

no-input replay of the exact bundled `LEVEL001.DAT` reached tick 9660,

was `7f4e6fd9b04f6fbcfa4fe3fa6a563458d928ea3ce08f1664a2d633767b5e8c96`.
The local original-result desktop capture at

From `05fed872bff69075331e4ca0d31f062cc33ce2b8` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 played the supplied L2 executable through a no-input
Beach level 01 run, **Quad Quirks on the Quay!**. The original level screen
showed 60 lemmings and a 9:00 limit. Fast forward was enabled after play
started; no skill was assigned. Its result screen said the tribe was extinct.
The native no-input run on the exact bundled `LEVEL010.DAT`, starting with
60 lemmings, reached tick 8100, released 60, saved 0 and lost 60. The source
level SHA-256 was `f8d7c88965a8301b552fbafe84d9d03afa8ba402369d5bb6509105f622869ce3`.
The ignored local capture
`.build/sequel-reference/l2.V1KLda/captures/beach-01-passive-result.png` has

This closes one L2 passive saved-count comparison. It does not compare

The L3 DOS run played the introduction automatically before the ark front
end. After the map-room selection it showed the Shadow Island level screen,
then a story transition before gameplay. The native app currently offers the
movies only in a manual gallery. This confirms an original automatic story
path for the start of a campaign, but does not establish all transition
triggers or soundtrack mappings.

At `96d1d84`, the bundled L3 `AUDIO/SFX001.IND` contains 40 little-endian
32-bit values. Read as successive sample lengths, they sum to 426,885 bytes;
`SFX001.RAW` contains 426,860 bytes, 25 fewer. The index and raw SHA-256
values are `1acca18e5f5e34266160359b715d939cf3037e2c00baf8573b8e442bd1d39f2b`
and `4059462ec4462c797feb842feffb41918f42f9279b590b65a5ea3e2d31d2d9f4`.
The values suggest clip boundaries, but their meaning, the short final segment,
playback rate and event mapping require original-engine validation. No native
sound binding was changed.

The supplied L3 data includes five original tutorial replay and text pairs,
`LEVEL995` through `LEVEL999`, under `LEVELS/REPLAY`. Their files
each contain 8-byte records when read as two little-endian 32-bit words.
The first word is monotonic in all five files; every stream
starts with `(0, 0xc0000001)` and ends with an event code `0x90000013`.
The files have 18 to 54 records. This describes their layout only. The event
codes, timebase, tool actions and outcomes have not been validated against
the original engine. These replays can support focused tool-rule work, but
they do not cover the 90 campaign levels.

From exact source revision `f13bc45289930ccef8ee0536103f4282e41253ee`
on 30 September, the bundled original tutorial text gave two nominal tool
rules. `LEVEL995.TXT` says a planted bomb explodes after five seconds, and
`LEVEL996.TXT` says a grenade bounces for eight seconds before exploding.
Their SHA-256 values were
`18d744b21fe8a4dbbf529f97982a6f095c68145d13bcc8794047d096886f5638`
and `5248ab04dbc6dac7112827c9f75a2dae139250d33524fc96a37f53580d7b865f`.
The native runtime uses 115 and 184 ticks respectively at its 23-tick
second, and its focused tests check both fuse boundaries. This supports the
nominal durations in bundled source text. It does not verify original-game
tick timing, grenade trajectory, blast effects or level outcomes. The
original-engine parity gate remains open.

The sequel screenshot script had a stale subset of shared sources and failed
to compile `ClassicSoundEffect`. It now builds the complete `NxlvKit` module,
as the other verification scripts do. A fresh render of bundled L2 Classic
level 02 succeeded and reported a 1280×160 level with eight skill slots.

## Packaged Apple silicon batch

On 30 September, the first isolated package build from
`68f0a37b7f7845dfb483ecba807a7019123ac37c` stopped in music bundling:
macOS system Python 3.9.6 does not provide `hashlib.file_digest`. A streaming
SHA-256 replacement passed an exact digest check and was committed at
`eab215e6e9fcd017c1484d2b134818975a15f144`. That build signed and
launched, but the bundled-resource test found missing Macintosh resource-fork
files. The copier had traversed zero files through the isolated worktree's
`Sources/Ports` symlink. Resolving that link before enumeration copied 24
resource forks in a targeted run, including the required Graphics and Levels
files. The copier fix was committed at
`7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333`.

A fresh build from `7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333` completed
through `Scripts/build-local-app.sh` with the supplied local assets. It is an
ad-hoc signed, arm64-only app on macOS 26.5.2, hardware model `Mac17,8`, and
is about 2.0 GiB. It includes 24 copied Macintosh resource forks and 495
music versions; 483 playback entries were re-encoded. `codesign --verify
--deep --strict` passed. The repository's empty-home launch smoke test passed
with the first frame at 2.091 seconds and music starting afterwards. The
bundled-resource test passed its L2 map, UI, cursor, sound, sprite and music
checks plus the all-in-one resource checks, including L3 data, NeoLemmix packs
and the recovered Macintosh forks.

The pre-existing L2 and L3 runtime test executables then passed against the
**packaged** `Ports/Lemm2` and `Ports/LEM3CD` directories. `NxlvKit` source
has no diff from the earlier source-suite revision
`66eac7dbfb60da288903bf9fe2803ede8d1267e2` to this package revision.
Those tests exercise all 120 L2 level load-and-advance paths and the 90 L3
level inventory, selected winning routes, campaign state and save validation.
They do not run inside the shipped app executable. The app executable SHA-256
is `4fc1bb1be84dc0fe14d1753d332155ebb98f822488affd55451a82fd1947520f`;
its bundled `libNxlvKit.dylib` SHA-256 is
`c3bbc0253b800b511cbea10266c3715464aecb0238ac1a83c22a69b54b32a168`.
The ignored local package is `.build/l2-l3-packaged/Ultimate Lemmings.app`.

This closes the local arm64 package build, signature, launch and bundled-data
checks at this source revision. It does not close packaged L2/L3 player
journeys, saved-run recovery and result identity, Intel coverage, physical
input and audio checks, notarisation or a frozen release candidate. The
packager also reported stale Trolley certificates; those targets remain
unverified in this package.

At `1b20a8871d26d983cb8ccd7d7594515c10f6a2ca`, the repository's
`sessions` app integration target passed with
`LEMMINGS_TEST_APP` pointing at this package. It checked saved Solo and Hot
Seat choices, checkpoint failure and recovery, history and Ready handovers,
plus playlist navigation across Classic, L2 and L3. It also passed the
saved-run, game and level identity guards for active and pending sequences.
This target compiles the app source into a test executable and reads the
package's resources. It is useful cross-game recovery evidence, but it does
not prove these journeys in the signed package executable or a continuous
native L2/L3 campaign.

## Signed app player and result sample

On 30 September, the signed arm64 package built from
`7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333` was exercised directly at
verification branch revision `4a50127b796e8029d9aa9e18596af1377b1d31d9`.
There was no app-code change between those revisions. It ran with an isolated
`CFFIXED_USER_HOME` at `.build/l2-l3-ui-home`; no existing player profile was
used. This was one Mac17,8 display and mouse/keyboard journey, not a hardware
matrix.

The package home screen showed `LEMMINGS 2 0/120 - PREVIEW` and
`LEMMINGS 3 0/90 - PREVIEW`. L2 Classic 1, “Do You Remember?”, loaded and
advanced with 60 lemmings. After a graceful app quit and relaunch, Resume
returned to that live level with its earlier clock state. L3 Classic 1 then
loaded and advanced with its original art and ten released lemmings. After a
second graceful quit and relaunch, Resume returned to L3 Classic 1 with its
clock and lemmings in progress. These are sample packaged player journeys and
graceful-relaunch recovery, not continuous campaigns or interrupted-process
recovery.

The isolated profile held separate L2 and L3 checkpoint envelopes. The L2
checkpoint had run ID `59DC9120-4AC1-44EB-8FDA-FB9421B4722C`, data set
`lemmings2` and tick 605. The L3 checkpoint had run ID
`5AD4DBC3-E6D8-4174-8DBF-F3C494327A9A`, data set `lemmings3` and tick
898; the two envelopes had distinct level fingerprints and state hashes. In
the resumed L3 run, the packaged End run action displayed a confirmation and
a Try again result with 0/20 rescued. The persisted Arcade record had the
same L3 run ID, `gameID: lemmings3`, `levelID: 1`, `didWin: false`, `saved: 0`
and a level fingerprint for that L3 level. The L3 checkpoint became an empty
checksum envelope after the result while the L2 checkpoint remained. A third
graceful relaunch then resumed the L2 checkpoint. Its packaged nuke control
produced a Try again result with 0/60 rescued. The second persisted Arcade
record had the original L2 run ID, `gameID: lemmings2`, `levelID: 0:0`,
`didWin: false`, `saved: 0` and the same L2 level fingerprint as its
checkpoint. Both checkpoints were empty checksum envelopes after their
results. These are two negative-result identity samples. They do not prove
winning-result identity, changed-content rejection, cross-build recovery or
the whole saved-run gate.

Full-screen captures and their SHA-256 manifest are kept locally under the
ignored `.build/sequel-reference/package-ui/` directory. The L2 resume image
hash is `37531ea0000cac4a6ada636b98947cfee14fd4c957be9ff6031298755418f495`;
the L3 resume image hash is
`8ff91fc53f786c7dcd2cafc5d82260033b8431055c1af285330f339a6c5f6dcc`;
the L3 result image hash is
`d62cabc1487ea7434af98a713e4daf485e965d3f2cf7b6aaf18556cecb1d66b8`;
the L2 result image hash is
`26e4d23a975adb2bff6703652a6651d1c68ebe3b37331ba17d6744045536b9bb`.
The app exited normally after each sample. Both games remain Preview.

## Bounded Highland route search

From `b534a0446d5648f73fca15c973fe2198bf54a5ae` on 30 September, the
existing L2 solver searched the previously untested Highland level 02 from
the 60 survivors of its recorded level 01 route. The direct solver command
used `--population 60 --beam 128 --budget 90`; there is no repository script
for a production solver run. The search expanded 68,883 states across 68,884
decision points in one 90-second round. Its best partial saved 0/60, and it
found no winning candidate. The only output is the ignored
`.build/l2-solver/highland02-60/highland-02.partial.json`, SHA-256
`f040c843321559c49e903946fb7f2f744e2beb4274e3045ec53ddf08c898edfc`.
The solver executable SHA-256 was
`741874750927adcaeda70212a1f98b03d2f56f5947388e32f35db85d6740aaac`;
there is no source diff in `NxlvKit` or `Tools/Lemmings2Solver` from the
packaged source revision. No route or fixture was promoted. The 47 missing
L2 routes, including Highland 02, remain open.

## Classic 026 route and movie-source check

From `98593e75ac527dbfe7cad2f5e155eb49bf924ccf` on 30 September,
the repository L3 solver searched the previously untested Classic level 026.
The command used `zsh Scripts/solve-lemmings3-levels.sh 026 --budget 90
--beam 128 --out .build/l3-solver/classic026-batch`. The solver widened
through three rounds to beam 512, expanded 1,683,039 states and found no
winning route. It reported a best partial with 0 saved and 0 lost. No
candidate or fixture was written. The solver executable SHA-256 was
`bb58ac83d51cf6dea48259755fad89d1eaf988195c37e1425937f7f57cba17ac`.
The 49 missing L3 routes, including Classic 026, remain open.

At the same source revision, a direct container inventory of the five supplied
L3 `.FLI` files found only video frames (`0xF1FA`) and palette/picture
subchunks (types 11, 12, 15 and 16). The files contain no audio chunk. The
supplied `AUDIO` tree has separate HMI tunes, Gravis patches and indexed raw
effects, but this inventory does not map them to any movie scene. The native
movie gallery currently plays decoded video without sound, and no automatic
story sequence was added. Original movie soundtrack and story timing remain
open pending a source-engine comparison and a verified cue mapping.

## L3 copied-fixture discovery pass

From `e0691a686712c16395ddda49a1d67cc80eb9ac2a` on 30 September,
`zsh Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh discover` ran with
`L3_COMPLETION_FIXTURES` set to an ignored copy of the 41 committed L3
fixtures. The repository manifest check passed at 273 campaign fixtures.
The tool replayed the 41 known L3 wins and tried its no-input and eight
rule-based policies against the uncovered levels. It found no new winning
route; the copied fixture directory remained byte-for-byte identical to the
committed directory. The L3 negative gate also passed. The verifier SHA-256
was `19404c46b0fbcf942c8f99906c6725b683ea7f5cc8aec73fe90bc9bc31e2625b`;
the ignored log at `.build/l3-discovery-2304/discover.log` has SHA-256
`a7952c0ae160579f3d78d68d77d8ca219788b72599fc7fbb46e9a08d94bd8945`.
This closes no new route or campaign gate. The 49 missing L3 fixtures and 47
missing L2 routes remain open. Do not repeat these discovery policies on the
same source and data; investigate original rules or a new guided route.

## Original L3 opening and Classic entry sample

From `95d6fef213eaa022b87a1006ffa6c90ecc119cb3` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 ran the supplied L3 CD executable in a fresh copied
reference directory, `.build/sequel-reference/l3.YkyQtl`. The executable
SHA-256 was `5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
The original played its introduction automatically before the ark screen;
Escape skipped the remaining introduction to the ark. Selecting the map room,
then Classic Island, showed its level 1 selector with 20 lemmings. Selecting
level 1 reached gameplay within about two seconds. No sustained `.FLI` movie
appeared between that level selection and gameplay. This narrows the observed
opening path; it does not establish the triggers for the other four movies or
all story screens.

The ignored full-screen captures of the introduction, ark, Classic selector
and gameplay have SHA-256 values
`c6a5a2002ac630c2d115a21688ea5adfa1e049620fe98fa0febdec86c59239aa`,
`61cc98b24305a7afccf4ec9485f437218f6816fa203f56e4167362ab2484128d`,
`d2deb49735601d3db9f5795ebc3e1a3d9a25270deee58c082c47d300db78645d` and
`50e6bbd314ae5295dbb8109a2bdd8be6302a75106689d59db407bc8bfc885963`
respectively. The reference configuration set `nosound = true`, so this run
provides no movie soundtrack or effect comparison. No tool action, matched
frame trace, winning route or ending was tested. The original-engine and
story/media gates remain open; both games remain Preview.

## Original L3 introduction audio reference

From `ce0410373bebc8b28a791357a81b56aceb990236` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 ran the supplied L3 CD executable from an ignored,
disposable copy at `.build/sequel-reference/l3-audio.j1TXIk/game`. The
supplied installer initially had Sound FX and Music OFF. In the copy only,
`INSTALL.EXE` selected Sound Blaster at port 220, IRQ 7 and DMA 1, enabled
both options, then saved a new `GAMES/L3/USER.DAT` (SHA-256
`0840da09977e09411e9078a367cc3d1189046e70ede8b2e5011f761d09dacdf6`).
The original supplied `USER.DAT` is unchanged (SHA-256
`f0a3a3e52df26657bbbb41be21af69ce5434a13b2f85cf1466f094f107f5bd8e`).
The game ran with `L3CD.EXE C:\GAMES\L3\`, `nosound = false`, and DOSBox's
`CONFIG -avistart` capture. No licensed data was added to Git.

The original introduction ran automatically and visibly displayed the
Classic Tribe and ark narrative while DOSBox recorded non-silent 48 kHz,
16-bit stereo PCM. The finalised `captures/video0009.avi` (SHA-256
`5634e654a673ed756c5845bc11849bf4b8e1828a3390608da9de618c91bf101e`)
contains 191.778 seconds of audio, with 15,441,786 non-zero samples,
RMS 1,038.14 and peak 7,515. The first non-zero sample is within the first
0.029 seconds. The paired introduction screenshot has SHA-256
`714ea60a15c1bfa504626a368117e1b63bf78b150cb447e61b9f04f0a3fc4a18`.
The earlier silent original capture used the supplied OFF settings and is
not evidence that this introduction is silent.

This establishes original-engine audio during the automatic introduction,
but does not yet identify its source cue, map its timing to `INTRO.FLI`, or
verify the other movies and story triggers. The native movie gallery still
plays decoded video without sound and does not start the introduction on the
original opening path. The story/media gate remains open. The strict route
counts remain L2 73/120 and L3 41/90; both games remain Preview.

## Original L3 introduction music isolation

From `bad2af77058c5d85df253b70f66e5dbacc99aba1` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 repeated the opening in the same ignored reference
copy. `INSTALL.EXE` kept Sound FX ON and changed only Music to OFF; the saved

`52358d988c19b221424f2ad6011e8db18ced21f6328c8b80afca04f05b594d86`.

were 4,911,198 non-zero samples, RMS 1,168.51 and peak 7,515. With Music

## Original L3 introduction cue identification

From `4f19f7a0f1a04cb86da13bfaa5bac74c18cbad00` on 30 September,
DOSBox Staging 0.83.0 repeated the first 15 seconds of the original
introduction with Music ON and Sound FX ON. In the ignored disposable game
copy only, valid Sound Blaster HMI files were substituted in bounded groups.
An unmodified repeat capture established the baseline. Replacing TUNE002,
then groups 003–006 and 007–008, and then TUNE009 did not materially change
the one-second audio RMS pattern. Replacing only `TUNE010.HMI` with the valid
`TUNE002.HMI` changed it strongly; an all-tune substitution did likewise.
The 15-value RMS-pattern correlation against the unmodified repeat was
0.9964 for 003–006, 0.9975 for 007–008, 0.9925 for 009, and -0.1690 for
TUNE010. This causal comparison identifies `AUDIO/SNDBLSTR/TUNE010.HMI` as
the original opening cue for the tested interval. Its original SHA-256 is
`3ed872df6cada2a7315af6ec9c4c1277e7d92c338fc92d5432d9a404c20f56a1`.

The ignored unmodified repeat and TUNE010-substituted AVI captures have
SHA-256 values `d78beab59cc64424b2e4c71c2ebbfc3b21d0a94f107490467fd3be514dda087c`
and `0726b3c66cc7d0bbb8410ac27dd02a58886cccaae3d2d5f0470db757afe51475`.
All HMI files in the disposable copy were restored; a directory comparison
against the supplied source shows only the earlier isolated `GAMES` install
directory. No licensed file was added to Git. The check does not establish
whether other cues begin later in the introduction, the exact cue start and
stop rules, or native HMI playback. The story/media gate remains open; both
games remain Preview.

## L3 Shadow levels 12 and 15 bounded searches

From exact source revision `ddabe07e89a1b8455b38884312b677d829b2e62c`
on 30 September, `zsh Scripts/render-sequel-shot.sh` rendered the bundled
Shadow levels 12 and 15 to ignored PNG files. The level data SHA-256 values
were `65ce26cd152e4b40535c68a938965e16548ef00299c34af29fa6fac06d3ea5fd`
and `973fb4edd55977ce4fa330f0c0bbbeb801e36dc4c27510c6211d699590ce5058`.
The PNG SHA-256 values were
`6cdd568e75ae3b056eaf91b3070dd67c54f08ce41e1c98c02b810ae29023befe`
and `7cbc82481ffbf6c41903b584d8e0dbc806ec951170004086e5d857bde5a83009`.
A temporary ignored geometry probe listed entrances, exits and pickups. No
repository script supplies those coordinates, so the probe compiled against
the repository's built `NxlvKit` module.

`zsh Scripts/solve-lemmings3-levels.sh 112 --budget 90 --beam 256
--fewer-inputs-first --out .build/l3-solver/shadow112-batch` reported
`UNSOLVED 112`. Its best partial saved 0 and lost 0. It counted 187 passive
decision points, searched to depth 227, widened across three rounds to beam
1024 and expanded 912,820 states in its reported 90 seconds.

`zsh Scripts/solve-lemmings3-levels.sh 115 --budget 75 --beam 128
--fewer-inputs-first --out .build/l3-solver/shadow115-batch` reported
`UNSOLVED 115`. Its best partial saved 0 and lost 0. It counted 262 passive
decision points, searched to depth 302, widened across four rounds to beam
1024 and expanded 1,360,734 states in its reported 75 seconds. Both searches
used solver executable SHA-256
`bb58ac83d51cf6dea48259755fad89d1eaf988195c37e1425937f7f57cba17ac`.
No candidate or fixture was written. These bounds do not establish that
either level is unsolvable.

A [Lemmings Chronicles walkthrough](https://www.cheatbook.de/wfiles/lemmingschronicles.htm)
mentions a grenade on Shadow level 12. The bundled level 112 has no grenade
pickup, so that route needs a source-version check. Its Shadow level 15 text
mentions prisoners, spades, a sucker and bricks, which are plausible guide
points for the bundled level 115. The guide is not replay proof. A later
search can test its early actions against the exact level before promotion.
Strict coverage remains L2 73/120 and L3 41/90. Both games remain Preview.

at a fallback decision instead of only the first one. This solver-only

rounds to beam 1024 and expanded 1,357,182 states in its reported 75

73/120 and L3 41/90. Both games remain Preview.

were 13 each for Classic 006 and Shadow 127, followed by Classic 007 at 17
and Classic 024 at 20. Classic 006, 007 and 024 already had bounded searches.

`5a5e4bf857d58c200e99f3d44f19ee18ceef298b36ecda7f79ed437e3046f1cf`.

received 60 seconds each with beam 128 and the same ranking. Level 211
reported 76 passive points, depth 116, four rounds to beam 1024 and 767,421

## L2 Highland level 02 cross-version guide prefix check

From exact source revision `4747d2a177490fc52eaaa2ad281644024611931b`
on 30 September, the repository renderer produced the supplied Highland 02
layout, `WEE BEASTIES !!!`, at 640×320. The local level is five minutes long
and has 30 Jumpers, 5 Platformers, 10 Runners, 1 Fencer, 4 Stackers, 10
Fillers, 20 Archers and 1 Stomper. Those title, time and skills match a
[GameFAQs walkthrough](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/pc/564598-lemmings-2-the-tribes/faqs/67370)
listed on a PC page. A source check from exact revision
`dfeb82954bab8503e090be083330b1693b9dc11e` confirmed that the guide
identifies itself as **Super NES** and warns that its levels differ from PC.
It describes a 40-lemming target while the supplied original DOS level starts
its tribe with 60. Treat its actions only as a cross-version route lead, not
as DOS engine evidence. The exact `LEVEL051.DAT` SHA-256 is
`0b2b4b9324e1239fcaf8f48cdd758257d7aba79f9867c2999c0992608ba719e5`.

A temporary ignored native probe assigned Runner to first lemming 0 at tick
60 and Jumper at tick 88. Both actions were accepted. The lemming crossed
part of the upper approach, reached `(239,144)` at about tick 140, then
turned back and became trapped. The guide's further jumps and Fencer action
have not been reproduced. A bounded sweep of second-Jumper timings 95–145
after that prefix did not put this lemming beyond x=240 above y=200 within
350 ticks. This checks only one native prefix and two timing windows; it does
not refute the walkthrough or prove a rule difference. The ignored render,
trace and second-jump log SHA-256 values are
`7050d497f6301f2db8a084d20d7a30c00673d997d0abcae6c09f70f1aa5c423a`,
`f722d75fcb33a9f8489a23944eceababfd878d2b9da461230561ea4f7a52ad8f`
and `d04c0840b4e3e9fa5682e2b0dd2a3e7ace8f717c8aa2d1595655f354fb3d86ef`.
No winning candidate or fixture was added. Highland 02's 60-survivor
carry-over and original-rule comparison remain open. Strict coverage stays
L2 73/120 and L3 41/90; both games remain Preview.

## L2 Highland level 02 guided-seed search

From exact source revision `0d67f499fc136655d7dd0dd0c59d451ba2c0e52f`
on 30 September, the repository L2 solver searched Highland 02 from a
level-bound, two-action seed: Runner to lemming 0 at tick 60 and Jumper at
tick 88. The seed SHA-256 was
`27f9081987a60a568c48fbac21756c30f31679a18aee5816624ab70e2c878bfb`.
The command used population 60, a 120-second budget, initial beam 128 and
reduced CPU priority. The solver executable SHA-256 was
`741874750927adcaeda70212a1f98b03d2f56f5947388e32f35db85d6740aaac`.

The pass reported `UNSOLVED highland-02`, best saved 0/60. It counted 88,776
decision points, expanded 88,775 states and completed one beam-128 round in
its reported 120 seconds. Its ignored partial trace kept the two guide
inputs but made its next accepted assignment only at tick 3096; it saved no
lemmings at the 4,500-tick limit. That partial SHA-256 was
`db019058c53c659c29d2a83044c2c917ee0429bf29e30ae2246d700fb1b19f89`.
This bounded result does not show that the route or level is impossible. It
suggests that the current beam ranking does not preserve useful early
follow-up actions to this prefix. No candidate fixture was written. Do not
repeat this seed and bound on unchanged source and data. Highland's chain,
original-rule comparison and ending remain open. Strict coverage stays L2
73/120 and L3 41/90; both games remain Preview.

## L2 Highland level 02 two-jump timing grid

From exact source revision `2bdbd7618d18245af81f7712bdf532d7b7ebd84e`
on 30 September, a temporary ignored native probe held Runner on lemming 0
at tick 60 and swept a first Jumper action at ticks 57–105 with a second
at least eight ticks later through tick 180. Of the bounded combinations,
2,132 second actions were accepted. None placed that lemming beyond x=239
while above y=200, or above y=120, by tick 250. The probe executable and
result-log SHA-256 values were
`9ae9481baf41d816c972b9dd342d063cd89c9a405a6df63f1dc89e270d5b8c38`
and `a616af9db96277377c7c65006c629adf7040171172957f9fa83c446f846011e8`.

and a Fencer, and source-rule parity is still unverified. No winning route
or fixture was added; Highland 02 and the 1.8 gate remain open.

## L2 Highland level 02 three- and four-jump prefix bounds

From exact source revision `16c522d68fb154bcbb5e3d992fb16b6ee03bbb54`
on 30 September, ignored native probes extended the Runner-at-tick-60 and
Jumper-at-tick-88 prefix. The three-jump grid swept the second Jumper at
ticks 96–180 and the third at least eight ticks later through tick 240.
It accepted 4,969 pairs. By tick 300, none moved lemming 0 beyond x=239
while above y=200; the furthest case used ticks 88, 98 and 165 and reached
`(239,128)`. A fourth Jumper sweep at ticks 173–290 after that case accepted
99 actions. Its best height was y=125 at x=198, and none exceeded x=239
while above y=200 by tick 330. The probe executable and result SHA-256 values
were `3859d374e3514833d45f7d9d2442ad7c883aea69e617d9d1c47cd7e61a756910`,
`1c12af1d7c80268ce9648e6358567438391f0aaaffe15547718523c8c1b7c538`,
`911b00e3cecb63b4974cbe14c2f0101618fde807b1b1a37af9` and
`ecf3e3e80cb1acd643c8700f30eb003b1a0d41df2a2607a9ee6a12d645f6d079`.
The fourth-jump bound is conditional on one three-jump prefix; neither
bound proves the level impossible. An independent
[Amiga walkthrough](https://www.lemonamiga.com/doc/lemmings-2-the-tribes/996)
also describes a Runner and several Jumps before crossing the chasm, but is
not DOS-engine evidence. No winning candidate or fixture was added. Do not
repeat these bounds on unchanged source and level data. Highland 02 and its
original-rule comparison remain open; strict coverage stays L2 73/120 and
L3 41/90. Both games remain Preview.

## 1 October source-built route rerun

At working-tree revision `54913345b32a6193d603a8b6af7cfeeeb892162b`, the
sequel engine and route fixtures had no local edits. Xcode 26.6 built the
current NxlvKit and the route verifiers from source, using its matching SDK
and writable module caches. The other working-tree edits were in difficulty
and learning artifacts and were left untouched.

The L2 gate replayed all 73 recorded routes and 27 carry-over variants twice.
Its malformed-evidence checks passed. Strict mode reported the same 47 missing
routes and only one complete tribe chain, Cavelems. L3 replayed all 41 recorded
routes twice. Its fixture manifest and malformed-evidence checks passed.
Strict mode reported 49 missing routes. Both strict gates remain open.

The current L3 discovery tool tried its nine fixed policies on all 49 uncovered
levels and found no new route. A source-built L3 beam solver then searched level
109 for 30 seconds with beam 128 and fewer-inputs-first ranking. It expanded
783,956 states and found no win. The source-equivalent solver binary also
searched levels 109, 110, 112, 115 and 117 with the same 30-second bounds, then
levels 109, 110 and 112 for 60 seconds with distance-first ranking. None found
a win. The latter searches expanded 1,214,631, 925,151 and 618,104 states.

The source-built L2 solver searched Egyptian 09 at population 60, beam 128 and
a 60-second budget. It expanded 51,315 states and found no win after 151 seconds
of elapsed time. All search outputs stayed in ignored `.build` directories.
These bounded searches do not show that a level is unsolvable. They add no
completion evidence. The strict coverage remains L2 73/120 and L3 41/90.
Both games remain Preview.

## Original Shadow 15 stair comparison

From exact source revision `1ca69a9d15aa4dedf2c97015784d5abea34377af`
on 1 October, DOSBox Staging ran a disposable original-game copy with Shadow
15 substituted into Shadow Island's opening slot. The copy paired the level,
permanent objects and temporary objects: the source and substituted SHA-256
values respectively matched at
`973fb4edd55977ce4fa330f0c0bbbeb801e36dc4c27510c6211d699590ce5058`,
`9369a67124d437efc9608f1cbddd69d360a5ce910e1a317a0cb96d687387342e`
and `b9c425e550418cba1e52c797aab2cf12d8238f7d2d70f9a7cd48b5bed0304e71`.
This is an altered opening-slot comparison, not a continuous campaign run.

The original level ran without a tool input. In the captured opening frame,
both extra lemmings were on the upper-right structures. Later frames show one
descending the right stairway. The ignored opening and later window captures
have SHA-256 `5c423baeb4d4ee8455f95564f65596c5587d067dccc9e32151aa607b81951d59`
and `2919d024bac0311461f96ae3adaf8db4b9c64838bf329b5d3acc2f01157476a1`.
No exact tick alignment, tool action, saved result or winning route was
recorded.

An ignored native no-input probe on the exact level ran to tick 1,200. Both
extras remained confined to their first ledges: lemming 0 at y=56 and
lemming 1 at y=104. Its log SHA-256 was
`0a9f1bff080839b7bc60ebffa8da69fe2209d325df53f524a71130affeab9c62`.
The native walker turns at x=247, y=56 and x=255, y=104 because the next
terrain rise is eight pixels while its current step limit is four. An ignored
prototype increased walker, swimmer and creature step limits to eight.
Its passive Shadow 15 probe picked up spades at tick 215 and moved the other
extra through the right stairs; the prototype library and log SHA-256 values
were `f51b18c5a7316251d7a13fbddfc99168ceaad7548850c48eb75f817cae52e242`
and `e63d12d429b1ecf5fb941299383451e1ccbd709c7873c9fab4269a44e0320ae8`.
Those extra changes were not isolated from the walker change. A tracked
walker-only trial failed `Scripts/run-lemmings3-runtime-tests.sh` at its
existing Classic 3 spade replay. It was reverted; the tracked tree and all
41 recorded L3 fixtures remain unchanged. The original stair path is a
specific rule lead, but a general eight-pixel step is not validated across
levels. L3 remains Preview.

At `3a80987`, a follow-up ignored collision probe found the changed Classic
3 path climbs eight-pixel cells at `(144,72)` and `(152,64)`, both tagged
`0x0020`, at ticks 123 and 131. Under the experimental rule its old guided
replay ends with 0 saved and 22 lost at tick 722. Shadow 15's first blocked
rise is also eight pixels, at `(248,48)` with tag `0x0060`; its second
prisoner reaches an eight-pixel rise tagged `0x0020` at `(256,96)`. The probe
log SHA-256 values were
`6426a37181acf3b8488cfa8d3efd0bd97206af85a825ff61a34d2ba19b846e6f`
and `6a301b6879f812ddf2570975e8616c0a392c208417f140db3fac11c34118562f`.
The tag alone does not separate the two levels. An original Classic 3 stair
comparison is needed before accepting or narrowing this rule change.

## Original Classic 3 opening-slot collision comparison

From exact source revision `6369e92666c4e06b483890f6f30db7f0076602e5`
on 1 October, DOSBox Staging ran a disposable original-game copy with the
supplied Classic 3 level and both object files substituted into an opening

The frame series below selected **Egyptian Island**, not Classic Island. Matching
replacement file hashes did not prove which island the original engine
rendered. The angel statue, colours and island map confirm the wrong selection.
The frames therefore provide no Classic 3 collision or step-rule evidence.
They remain listed only to identify the invalid batch; do not use them for
parity or release decisions. This altered opening slot also cannot prove a
continuous Classic campaign or its carried population. Ignored frames 8, 10,
12 and 14 have SHA-256 values
`f1df473c05e6ab86cdd5c37043813f4cb034138d20ee19e528b365823722f57a`,
`1282839fb6159e11787033c07366a6f3df894a9d2635e716543e73fbba3bb435`,

and `ceb905ada74a10e9562be8dd4c79ee94886f491da2cfcab0b92c3b32d267e74e`.

A bespoke ignored source-layer probe, used because no repository script
reports object contributions at one collision cell, found that the disputed

background is also `0x1000`. The diagnostic log SHA-256 is
`a146d271beb349313f1aef25c987924c96c00b39483edefded71a46a552bd9f2`.
The source-layer observations do not establish original-engine behaviour.
The invalid Egyptian frame series cannot accept or reject an eight-pixel step
rule. A new reference run selected Classic Island explicitly and confirmed its
grey and green level artwork. Its disposable `LEVEL001.DAT`, `PERM001.OBS` and
`TEMP001.OBS` match the supplied Classic 3 files byte for byte at the hashes
above. In a no-tool replay, the original lemmings approach the central raised
platform and one reaches its top. Ignored frames 18, 24, 28 and 30 have

`31512555ee077f1b36699befc7c50eedd19da10d92b6338beb602bcdfb068012`,

`ed1dfb3c5db5de12d368c66641dde5a6fb5d548f92290703c4686359b1b9397b`.
Against the same supplied level, the tracked native runtime built by
`Scripts/run-lemmings3-runtime-tests.sh` passes its current runtime suite,

The original and native no-tool paths therefore diverge at this structure.
A tighter frame sequence shows the unassisted original walker on the first

27 and 29 have SHA-256 values
`e26425db7719739a0d5a0a8aa966903c1bdbdca4591cda07727306df99104b9a`,
`883318718fa292afa158e358cc668fdc5c933f61aeb5ac1f23ece3b992126b34` and

The original walker negotiates these two eight-pixel rises without a tool.
This does not establish the exact tick rule or whether every walker may take

An ignored native prototype reversed the decoded directions of extra
lemmings 10006 and 10007 without changing the four-pixel step limit. In

still oscillated on their initial ledges through tick 1,200. The prototype
library and log SHA-256 values are
`4e36cd359fd9378d3b327760777c75e8f0ff0d3786443263b814b8579f7d2196`
and `1a239a430395e2f009e3045fff18ae7f1666ef3fdeda28a21f6c67ead6201e9a`.

## Walker-only eight-pixel trial and replay impact

From exact source revision `ff90ead874a7358be2223953d3338cbf2cc995b8`
on 1 October, an ignored prototype changed only the walking lemming's maximum
step rise from four to eight pixels. The repository has no script for building
a substituted single-source prototype, so `swiftc` built it from the current
`Sources/NxlvKit` sources with an ignored copy of `Lemmings3Runtime.swift`.
The prototype library SHA-256 is
`d32a986816da2ce523d9d8a6b33d480d4253097a231163a3d8d6d0520253b9e7`.
Swimmer and creature step limits remained four. This isolates the original
Classic 3 walker observation; it does not prove the general rule.

The repository solver, linked to this prototype, found an isolated Classic 3
route with four inputs, 10 saved, none lost and 10 in reserve at tick 465.
It replayed the exact level twice. Its log and candidate SHA-256 values are
`850cf74534b9dcf140fb11039d31924ffb74a6167b988dba799df3a7893a2494`
and `902e64475fa09413b9748d864dec074622157f3212497537289370ff0a230aa6`.
An ignored carry-over probe applied the same inputs to a 22-lemming
population, matching the count after Classic 2, and got 10 saved, none lost and 12 in reserve at tick 465; its log
SHA-256 is `b3281f0e330e3d3fa09d352bf56bbce9142eccb2db23688494c66f23799bc219`.
A temporary tracked trial installed this fixture and refreshed the manifest.
`Tools/CampaignCompletion/report.py --check` passed all 273 fixture hashes,
including 41 L3 routes, but the L3 completion gate stopped at Classic 4:
its old replay input 4 was rejected at tick 517. The trial changes were
restored after validation.

A selected-fixture replay audit under the prototype found 17 of 41 recorded
L3 routes passing and 24 failing. Its log SHA-256 is
`3d9dd9fb450d3671ae4b6a2f4ca7c012974d84c606946d7c67c64fff2249b4f6`.
The solver searched each failed route with a 45- or 60-second budget, initial
beam 128 and fewer-inputs-first ranking. It found twice-replayed replacement
candidates for 15 of the 24, including Classic 4, Classic 13 and Egyptian 17.
Classic 13 improves from one saved to 13 saved with no loss; Egyptian 17
needs no input under the trial and improves from 10 retained to 19. A
separate ignored repair pass discarded failed inputs from old routes, kept
only outcomes with at least the old retained population and replayed each
candidate twice. Combined, the two methods produced candidate routes for
17 of the 24 invalidated fixtures. The solver and repair logs have SHA-256
`8350b4b5d0f6b489aaf7d40a7ca6d9ba8bf5742ad00e103202a5c35f85cabe6b`
and `916673e5a7c5a223edda1ab341562cc84b96512e07dcc4c3eb88308b2600fbf2`.
These are prototype candidates in ignored build data, not accepted fixtures.

Seven invalidated fixtures still have no replacement: Classic 18, 19 and 21;
Shadow 8; and Egyptian 3, 22 and 26. Their bounded searches found no win;
no result establishes that the levels are impossible. The walker change is
not retained because the full recorded L3 corpus no longer passes. The
tracked runtime, fixture and manifest are restored, and the baseline manifest
passes. No new strict route or exit gate closed. L2 remains 73/120, L3 41/90;
both stay Preview.

## Next verification batch

Use the ignored walker-only candidates as seeds to repair the seven remaining
invalidated routes, and compare the step rule on further original levels
before changing the tracked runtime. If the parity rule is accepted, replay
all 41 routes twice, update their fixture hashes and run the full strict gate.
Keep isolated route wins separate from the continuous 22-lemming Classic
campaign and ending evidence. Do not repeat these bounded solver passes on
unchanged source and data.

## Seeded route repair and terrain-filter trial

At exact source revision `2932c998e5f63ecc26ea516cb015156d2a5ff735`
on 1 October, a bounded timing-shift pass used the seven unmatched
walker-only trial fixtures as seeds. It retained only winning outcomes with at
least the recorded survivor count and replayed each candidate twice on its
exact level. Provisional candidates were found for Classic 19 (1 saved,
1 retained, 47 inputs, 830 trials), Shadow 8 (1 saved, 1 retained, 17 inputs,
82 trials) and Egyptian 3 (1 saved, 11 retained, 132 inputs, 8 trials).
Their ignored JSON SHA-256 values are
`5c320c90aafc59fe55e0af44affb796b6cc4615a813b795eef771cbe50fc2625`,
`42d55dcd68cc520c7849bd768a5c5920d9f1335f8a9827d4bd98ca3a305176b7`
and `1b7cbf48e8275badb2d9586ed2194e79edeca15d7461b20af3a339a854a698e0`.
The combined search log SHA-256 is
`4f5d0344e79d6b39218d53596a50c07b9317fbcc6ec862c1adac200bcad9970b`.
Classic 18, Classic 21, Egyptian 22 and Egyptian 26 remained unmatched after
265, 3,409, 481 and 121 timing trials respectively. These are bounded

1,584,593 for Classic 21, 1,698,958 for Egyptian 22 and 1,395,826 for
Egyptian 26. Only Classic 21 produced a twice-replayed win: 2 saved,
0 reserve, 18 lost, 158 inputs at tick 3,213. This retains one fewer lemming
than its recorded fixture, so it is not an acceptable replacement. The ignored
search log SHA-256 is
`401c7bc89af54cce6ebc74872acc4d652dbe891eccbee5cc8c1e29168f3f5f4f`.
Do not repeat either search bound on unchanged source and data.

For Egyptian 26, the supplied scene has a rise from y=144 terrain tagged
`0x0060` to y=136 terrain tagged `0x2020` at x=160. The unrestricted eight-pixel
prototype moves its first lemming beyond that rise and loses all 20 by
native tick 589. The tracked four-pixel runtime keeps the first lemming near
x=152–159; its passive run ends at tick 2,760 with 0 saved, 10 lost and
10 in reserve. The original game was run in disposable DOSBox Staging with
Egyptian 26 files substituted into the Egyptian opening slot. The map label
and level art were inspected, and the substituted level, permanent-object and
temporary-object files matched the supplied files at SHA-256 values
`f81b27c79f425a143c7316e1d45fbe4436cacd23f1aedf378e1454cb757636ba`,
`5d4b76e0c625f50a186ab2696ddb2febaffeb5c9d06ae40e30f0882ccc3412d3`
and `4fb0d0881ff1f01f8e03567c83e22f30a51a1eb30a32bf1a7d8d3cb5934158ea`.
Original no-tool frames 15 and 25 have SHA-256 values
`716abe810b3e195b99abfceddb5bd55ad10cd277d7bac6986ccc7e962600fa27`
and `cb8ca83111244b98a80ffaa2fa6fb6c9eb8ef5187fb25ea972c8e230a567cdba`.
The captured frames do not establish exact tick or position parity.

An ignored prototype permitted eight-pixel walker rises except when the
landing cell has terrain bit `0x2000`. Its library SHA-256 is
`bcfff968f5be0716084be6687869e64c5fa6f8e8894e10364ae8e1165ee2cb5c`.
Egyptian 26's passive result matched the tracked four-pixel runtime, but its
recorded winning route still failed. Across all 41 recorded L3 fixtures,
22 passed and 19 failed; Classic 3 also failed at its first input. The audit
log SHA-256 is
`dc52c40061d6962dec47b59993aedf74199927b6ce073c81e60ea1f2637b6081`.
This filter does not establish the original step rule and is not retained.

No provisional route was added to the manifest or accepted as a verified new
route. The tracked source, fixtures and manifest are unchanged. The full L3
replay, original-engine, continuous-campaign, packaged-app and hardware gates
remain open; L2 remains 73/120 and L3 remains 41/90. Both stay Preview.

## L2 Sports 08 bound and Egyptian 26 image inspection

From exact source revision `faa107abc894986a2fa2db485f83d86bc841d822`
on 1 October, the repository L2 solver searched the previously untested

`426a25ff8afed40f6a0988a364e88570da01e11b85fb3e4a7425ccb6828d7355`,
`741874750927adcaeda70212a1f98b03d2f56f5947388e32f35db85d6740aaac`,
`aa0aa04e23042c176f7ddb573fab855e8724f7c868cf7753b0020812ed071ad5`
and `2ea9f85aa9b8c7f01873182d47b62aa22d1209109e7fdd8c9cc4caae9f95dfd8`.
This bound does not prove Sports 08 impossible. Do not repeat it on unchanged
source and data.

The original Egyptian 26 no-input frame series was inspected again. Its
source level header declares zero extra lemmings. Frame 15 shows a lemming
on the narrow central pillar; frame 25 shows a lemming beyond the central
rise on the right-hand structure. As no tool was assigned and no extras were
defined, the original game permits a walker to pass that structure by some
unassisted movement. This observation weighs against filtering out the
`0x2020` eight-pixel rise solely by terrain tag. The frames do not identify
an exact walker, tick or collision coordinate, so the precise rule remains
open. The ignored terrain-filtered prototype is still rejected. No runtime,
fixture, manifest or status label changed. L2 remains 73/120 and L3 remains
41/90; both stay Preview.

## Verified L2 Outdoor 04 route

From exact source revision `d060eb9a1df4524cd7f886d2d5f3fe1dde5e1d91`
on 1 October, the already-built repository solver searched the previously
uncovered Outdoor level 04 with a fresh 60-lemming population, initial beam
128 and a 45-second reported budget. The selected-level repository script
would rebuild the unchanged library, so the compiled solver was called
directly. It found a seven-event route at tick 2,775, saving one of 60 for
Bronze. The search expanded 19,330 states. The exact source level SHA-256 is
`ef251a14bb922a1bf105c47637a5a8e6e532050a40d2a8254e0851409f388b5c`;
the candidate and search log SHA-256 values are
`1fb14e0a7daf731b4bc74c0fbcd9eff308c2f6e7e6d0eb2c56c06fb7644a5d38`
and `d7f58116b72cfbfda31b5dd1b4a22778222f44d87020bd8fd5ac96d68f3ebb00`.

The solver's promotion command replayed the exact level twice with matching
state hashes, then wrote `Tests/Lemmings2CompletionTests/Fixtures/outdoor-04.json`.
The fixture hash is the candidate hash above. The L2 evidence manifest was
regenerated and `Tools/Lemmings2Completion/report.py --check` passed all
74 fixture hashes and 27 carry-over variant hashes. The repository's full
non-strict L2 completion script passed its event and negative-gate checks,
replayed all 74 routes twice, and recorded Outdoor 04 as 1/60 Bronze at
tick 2,775. Its log SHA-256 is
`6c8b294dc668529ba9f948717097c359cda4e34e8bd1d642ce34d661bca5bdc5`.
The strict condition was then run with the exact gate binary built from this
source. It replayed the same 74 routes, reported 46 missing and only
Cavelems fully chained, and failed as required. The strict log SHA-256 is
`bf2b36196008e0a6550bbee28c767c10752b425a256068b819609e9fd285c359`.

Outdoor 04 now has a verified standalone route. The current Outdoor 03 chain
passes only one survivor, so this 60-lemming route does not extend the
continuous Outdoor tribe run beyond level 03. L2 coverage is 74/120, L3
remains 41/90, eleven L2 chains remain incomplete, and both games stay
Preview. Original-engine parity, campaign endings, packaged player journeys
and hardware evidence remain open.

## Signed app interrupted-process recovery sample

On 1 October, at verification source revision
`8cced50e63ab65be0100e14271de7a71d069a80d`, the signed arm64 package
built from `7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333` passed a focused L2
interrupted-process recovery check on macOS 26.5.2, Mac17,8. The package's
app and runtime source had no diff from its build revision. Its executable
SHA-256 was
`4fc1bb1be84dc0fe14d1753d332155ebb98f822488affd55451a82fd1947520f`.
`codesign --verify --deep --strict` passed again.

A fresh isolated `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` at `.build/l2-l3-crash-home` opened L2
Classic 01, “Do You Remember?”, in the signed package. The app saved a
checksum-valid checkpoint for run
`31376B66-E9BC-4FB6-AF1B-4082E4206E71`, data set `lemmings2`, level index
0, tick 1,581 and state hash
`41ba50742f7ff43ae5b98d2a2d49a17625a07b5b7e44f4e8302e2e9639b80d98`.
The app process was then terminated with `SIGKILL`; this did not use the
normal quit or checkpoint shutdown path. The same signed package reopened
with the same isolated profile and offered Resume. Resume restored the L2
level and its earlier 3:15 clock state, paused. After unpausing, the same
run ID advanced to tick 1,758. Its checksum-valid checkpoint had state hash
`2c68f99ae16a4a763f188e2c50610c81a863f8c1a7f0b6c2d9762a41bcdf1e30`.
The before and after checkpoint file SHA-256 values were
`d93fb505369db6711d25260f53868174e13483b0ee0e4b9049a26789de43841a`
and `818c4199ed286d112ff58ebd0247b508d850acde3445b429b379192999854822`.

The ignored local screenshots are under `.build/l2-l3-crash-home`.
`relaunch-home.png` shows Resume and both sequel Preview labels, SHA-256
`02d397bb0bc72be5b14ca99ed4630fe1486f94ca654ba54dbfc2d64695a1ceeb`.
`resume-click.png` shows the restored level, SHA-256
`919ae5d23b64d071a17c6c3f8a3cf6c0cd9e9c8047857a9fda44fe5c283f3d38`.
`resumed-running.png` records the continued run, SHA-256
`5bea0651b5d684507db9f277561e131b54961afe21d52358e94fe71ae3850ee5`.

This closes one signed-package L2 interrupted-process recovery sample.
L3 interruption, changed-content rejection in the package, cross-build
recovery, winning-result identity, continuous campaigns, original-engine
parity and the wider hardware matrix remain open. L2 is still 75/120 and L3
is still 41/90. Both games remain Preview.

## Packaged-resource sequel app integration batch

On 1 October, the repository's `Scripts/run-sequel-mac-artwork-tests.sh` ran
against `.build/l2-l3-packaged/Ultimate Lemmings.app` resources on the arm64
Mac17,8. The initial run from exact source revision
`40eb0a2125cb11391afa2673231f0956852f76f8` passed the engine and visual
stages but stopped when the app test failed to link Sparkle. The script now
finds the pinned Sparkle framework and links it, as the other app test scripts
do. The next run reached the app tests but exposed a test fixture that reused
a settings window after changing its preset. A fresh settings window now
checks the Modern default. The two failed ignored logs have SHA-256 values
`5b0a7b43248330859dad630b0e3b0fd6f4c775cff367f582663cfa8f05caa6a8`
and `6a32cb40431437f44218883edd9552a9897b99d9da75fb375248f1da63f2d215`.
Neither failure was a sequel gameplay result.

With those test-only fixes committed at exact source revision
`55f5c69f4302a440e986b9147a81c51e181fc305`, the full repository
sequel artwork and app integration script passed against the package's
bundled resources. The app test executable SHA-256 was
`9fb8ee803b9b40c6f09ebc089145d14554bacc77761924102f28d98bbe675253`.
The ignored final log SHA-256 was
`dd960fd1efafb1a83906183a6c095ec6c379ac5dc8bd86c942d16ae9fcdc6047`.
The data stage checked all twelve L2 styles, three L3 styles, 120 checked-in
visual hashes and 59,542 frames across 214 levels. The app target passed
L2 native completion and single-recorded rescue and skill counts, L3
minimum-clear and single-recorded result checks, both sequel checkpoint
round trips with exact paused restoration and continued state, invalid
journal rejection, original media playback checks, and control and artwork
checks.

This closes the source app integration target against the packaged content.
The target compiles current app source into a separate test executable. It
does not prove a winning result in the signed package executable, continuous
campaigns, original-engine parity, cross-build recovery or the hardware
matrix. The signed package still uses source revision
`7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333`; its runtime and app source
have no diff from this verification revision. L2 remains 75/120 and L3
remains 41/90. Both games stay Preview.

## Signed app changed-content recovery refusal

At exact verification source revision
`062457539d3864e0073a9fc1d9c4f9388871b9a2` on 1 October, the signed
arm64 package built from `7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333`
was tested with two disposable profiles and copied sequel data. The package
and original licensed data were not changed. `codesign --verify --deep
--strict` still passed after the checks.

For L2, a copy of the packaged `Lemm2` data changed one title byte in
Classic 01. The exact level SHA-256 changed from
`2be6334f0423ee3ac635946ee855aa8caa4bc8cdd53190c6f16d8cc7d806cc8e`
to `3821cded2521570b9780906ebe4e4b2e570bf04ba0872d1bb7e63c9226fa191f`.
A copied, checksum-valid checkpoint for run
`31376B66-E9BC-4FB6-AF1B-4082E4206E71` pointed to that data copy. Its
SHA-256 was
`e28acc164d5494b3d39f9ae1034886eb9be9555b498e2cd9919a98543974e607`.
The signed package offered Resume, then showed “Cannot restore run” and a
different game data or rules message. It did not enter the changed level.
The checkpoint retained the same bytes and valid envelope checksum. The
ignored home and refusal screenshot SHA-256 values were
`87d672f538c8f6df69e830f53538aa3a6819d40d51285200944344a3d89f11ff`
and `ac56739118f4ac59fc6230aee897e6757132abe274406d1fbb4c6f1c9625a75f`.

For L3, a separate copy of the packaged `LEM3CD` data replaced Classic 01's
30-byte level record with the valid Classic 02 record. The exact Classic 01
level SHA-256 changed from
`7f4e6fd9b04f6fbcfa4fe3fa6a563458d928ea3ce08f1664a2d633767b5e8c96`
to `bc269a77ebb2d145ee18b0cf2a6b03d6019043c35094dfd57f879e1da0bf92f2`.

## Signed-package L3 winning-result identity sample

At exact verification source revision
`e2b1daf085c78b0bb1da2d2c9ffe0ab7c4de79e6` on 1 October, the signed
arm64 package built from `7352629e300c033ecdbc63d1c9dd854204df9333`
was run with a disposable copy of the earlier isolated profile. Its executable
SHA-256 remained
`4fc1bb1be84dc0fe14d1753d332155ebb98f822488affd55451a82fd1947520f`;
`codesign --verify --deep --strict` passed. No player profile or game data
outside the ignored test copy was changed.

The checked-in Classic 01 L3 route (`001.json`, SHA-256
`5ac07c6d2841583d3112544bceb401f70c791b4a146891964c84b6d33b595841`)
was used to create a checksum-valid checkpoint at tick 450. Its ten Walker
inputs all precede that tick. A local ignored helper (executable SHA-256
`2404bab34c51a6386029b07a141a6675585befd74b2686eb897c7a05e50b4d38`)
checked the exact level and initial-state hashes, replayed the input journal,
restored the generated checkpoint through the source recovery model and
confirmed the expected 10-saved, 0-lost, 10-reserve outcome at tick 482. The
seeded checkpoint SHA-256 before launch was
`d1ca0304286e6f588a532714807887b7325baf0542e384815e850193bccb0aac`.

The signed app's default Resume action opened the L3 play window for run
`704BE369-0EBA-4876-A000-0821FE2DCC1C`. After unpausing, the app finished
the level and cleared that run's checkpoint to a valid empty envelope,
SHA-256 `62d503834336854198aac7fd8a8ed013d818c30b162f26ad31e28844e95d649f`.
The copied Arcade file had no completed attempts before this run. Afterwards,
it had exactly one attempt under the same run ID: `gameID: lemmings3`,
`levelID: 1`, `didWin: true`, `saved: 10`, `walker: 10`, `released: 10`,
`engineLost: 0`, `reserve: 10`. Its level fingerprint begins with the fixture's
exact level SHA-256
`7f4e6fd9b04f6fbcfa4fe3fa6a563458d928ea3ce08f1664a2d633767b5e8c96`.
The copied Arcade file changed from SHA-256
`e802ecf0a48e4ec44f4f5f8d7fdc665401bef23c1b32c3f6eb2f975b494dcf24`
to `9e9940aa5fe25e8179f1443a7de2506720b80b6788dd9cbb44ae93fcd00537a7`.

This closes one signed-package L3 winning-result and run-identity sample from
a seeded, verified checkpoint. It does not show the whole route entered by a
player in that package, an L2 winning result, a continuous campaign, an ending,
original-engine parity or the hardware matrix. L2 remains 75/120 and L3
remains 41/90. Both stay Preview.

## Inconclusive signed-package L2 winning-result attempt

check. The Classic 01 fixture SHA-256 was

A local ignored helper (executable SHA-256
`24c3cda97d773d4e6b95aa993de959ee7c3a10250360d7dfd92e17dd868d2cdf`)
replayed that exact level twice, confirmed the same 60-save result at tick
3,074, converted its events to the app recovery journal, and restored the
generated checkpoint through the source recovery model at tick 3,050. The
seeded checkpoint SHA-256 before package launch was
`a1684f20df6abf7b8efd9b85e364cd18b656f488e798d680faac805336295ca9`.

The signed package opened the L2 play window for the same run ID,
`31376B66-E9BC-4FB6-AF1B-4082E4206E71`. The targeted Space and single-step
keys did not advance the persisted tick from 3,050. A subsequent attempt to
activate the package for a focus-checked global Space key did not make it the
frontmost app, so no global key was posted. The package process later exited
without a result. The disposable Arcade file still had zero completed
attempts; its SHA-256 was
`2191e6963a5269644104d2b40b8df595c29608733a8af534faab69e399248c9d`.
The remaining checkpoint was still for the same run at tick 3,050, SHA-256

This confirms that the signed package accepted the verified L2 checkpoint,

## Interrupted L3 Classic 028 wider bound

At exact source revision `e10cc9ec94a8cb3adc35d7496abdc8f4734cfa9d` on
1 October, the repository L3 solver script rebuilt the unchanged solver and
started a 60-second, beam-128 search for uncovered Classic 028. The prior
recorded pass on this level used a 30-second budget. The executable SHA-256
was `5a5e4bf857d58c200e99f3d44f19ee18ceef298b36ecda7f79ed437e3046f1cf`.
The new process was still running after 36 minutes despite its reported
60-second budget. It was interrupted with SIGINT. Its ignored log was empty
(SHA-256 `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`),
and its candidate output directory was empty. No result, replay candidate or
fixture was produced. This attempt is an interrupted search, not an unsolved
result or evidence of a level defect. The solver checks time at beam and node
boundaries, while individual candidate advancement has no time check; future
searches need an enforced wall limit before this wider bound is retried. The
strict totals remain L2 75/120 and L3 41/90. Both games stay Preview.

## Enforced L3 solver wall bound

At exact source revision `661b4b0c1921f96650c770737b0bee56fc672f13`
on 1 October, the repository L3 solver script gained an outer process timeout.
It permits the requested budget plus 15 seconds per requested level, then
kills the solver and exits with status 124. This bounds candidate advances
that miss the solver's internal node-boundary time checks. The `missing`
selector uses a conservative 90-level ceiling. Compilation occurs before the
bound and is not included in it.

`zsh -n` and `git diff --check` passed. A disposable slow executable in ignored
build output exceeded a one-second requested budget; the wrapper stopped it
at its 16-second wall bound, printed `TIMEOUT` and returned 124. The same
script rebuilt the unchanged solver (SHA-256
`5a5e4bf857d58c200e99f3d44f19ee18ceef298b36ecda7f79ed437e3046f1cf`)
and completed a one-second, beam-8 smoke run on the already-covered Classic
001. It returned 0 with a 10-saved candidate in ignored output (log SHA-256
`866e094a4e6cd25edf1b109cc69dfc9897d04d0b43a95f323b2a6779dd0d23dd`).
The smoke candidate was not promoted or counted as new route evidence; it was
not given the two exact-level replays and manifest check required for a route.
The wrapper does not make a stuck compiler bounded, and the conservative
multi-level ceiling may permit a long individual level in a batch. New route
searches should use one level per invocation. The strict totals remain L2
75/120 and L3 41/90. Both games stay Preview.

## L3 supplied cue metadata inventory

At exact source revision `33cbac08b05fb74677a7fa718933799a967e699e`
on 2 October, a read-only inventory of the supplied DOS CD data checked the
Gravis tune configuration files and `L3CD.EXE` (SHA-256
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`).
The executable contains a numbered Sound Blaster HMI path format and
literal names for all five `.FLI` movies. Each numbered Sound Blaster HMI tune
from 002 through 010 has a Gravis `.INI` with the same number. The supplied
INI heading names 002–003 Classic, 005–006 Shadow, 007–008 Egyptian, 009
FrontEnd and 010 Intro. File 004 has no heading, so its role is unknown.

These are labels in supplied data, not playback traces. The earlier DOSBox
substitution independently confirms TUNE010 for the tested introduction
interval. The other tune labels do not establish a movie cue, its timing,
soundtrack implementation or an automatic story trigger. No asset or fixture
was changed. L2 remains 75/120 and L3 remains 41/90; both stay Preview.

## L3 Shadow 127–128 bounds and numeric argument fix

At exact source revision `8520cbf26a7d43a1230bd86f3ea7eb0196f3d8fb`,
the repository L3 solver script was asked to search the previously untested
Shadow levels 127 and 128 with a 45-second budget per level and beam 128.
It searched only 127, expanding 338,486 states over four rounds to beam
1,024, and found no win. The log SHA-256 was
`24472d29c47f6b76723ea743f7610e13f69697592e569396e01b9c7130869e41`;
the pre-fix solver executable SHA-256 was
`68ff83acd16e519961f6f4347dbabf332fb424107373cda8ee0d088ec819330a`.
Level 128 was skipped because the CLI removed every argument equal to any
option value, including the positional `128` matching `--beam 128`. It was
not counted as searched in that batch.

The CLI now skips only the value immediately after a valued option. At exact
fixed source revision `a1123dd4aee224b84b6a3d90af8a95efd04c1a09`, the
same repository script searched Shadow 128 under beam 128 and a 45-second
budget. It expanded 1,029,883 states over three rounds to beam 512 and
found no win. The fixed solver executable SHA-256 was
`aeeb979e1586a0a4924f4c653efbc93b9f473d4179cdb41d78c36e0737fa9360`;
the log SHA-256 was
`b4ca491dc24f24b7d51acdf30b2266353b3ff7dd330f57a8b39e426213d40ef8`.
This direct run validates that a requested numeric level is still selected
when its number equals a numeric option value. No candidate, fixture or
manifest changed. Do not repeat these exact bounds on unchanged source and
data. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both games stay Preview.

## L3 introduction audio asset check

At exact source revision `c722bad8d3455c16f35f8e70ce19be88bc2334be`
on 2 October, `afinfo` inspected the ignored local DOS OPL2 `01 Intro.m4a`
and the AAC copy in the signed packaged app. Both report 267.982 seconds of
44.1 kHz stereo audio. Their SHA-256 values are
`78d9760aecfdba1eedcd8a13ebf15b4903f5cdec49d4213693de89ef0753cc54`
and `1944653c46b6815c0b0f837c6175e0cd4307ca38a4489388eddea0e2a163b600`.
The source package calls this track “Intro” and lists a 2:10 loop; the native
catalogue labels it `lemmings3.intro`. Its packaged presence does not prove
that it reproduces the original game's identified `TUNE010.HMI` cue or that
it starts, loops and stops at the correct story frames. The original
`INTRO.FLI` header's nominal duration is 128.2 seconds. No native playback
was changed until cue identity and timing can be compared with the original
capture. Story audio, automatic transitions and the full media gate remain
open. Strict coverage stays L2 75/120 and L3 41/90; both games stay Preview.

## L3 introduction recording matched to original cue

At exact source revision `6619ba0cb6ed1e02f014884b4fee6bf049f3cec7`
on 2 October, the original Music ON DOSBox capture was compared offline with
the packaged DOS OPL2 `01 Intro.m4a`. The original AVI SHA-256 was
`d78beab59cc64424b2e4c71c2ebbfc3b21d0a94f107490467fd3be514dda087c`;
the packaged Intro AAC SHA-256 was
`1944653c46b6815c0b0f837c6175e0cd4307ca38a4489388eddea0e2a163b600`.
`faad` decoded the packaged recording. A local RIFF reader extracted the
AVI's `01wb` 48 kHz stereo PCM chunks. Comparing 50 ms RMS windows across
the first 49.95 seconds gave a Pearson correlation of 0.917 at a -0.15 s
offset, versus 0.821 at zero offset. As a negative control, the packaged
`02 Frontend.m4a` (SHA-256
`503ab25cf6a2d16bfd70d70d745ded8cef808acd44d8276bb5ccf7dbd25ae9b5`)
reached only 0.040 over the same ±1 s offset range. The ignored comparison
script SHA-256 was
`26b726425fdd38a346fc7f93d1ee547e87e465308562256ac08595d9cf257075`;
its ignored result log SHA-256 was
`3d2d91ef60bb2897bcd600396394b453fd93a40580050a8e749ed263f9fd26b7`.

Together with the earlier causal `TUNE010.HMI` substitution, this identifies
the packaged Intro recording as a rendition of the original opening cue for
the tested interval. Different sound hardware and encodings preclude a
sample-exact claim. The comparison does not establish the cue's full length,
loop point, stop event, other movie cues, or native playback synchronisation.
The gallery remains silent and manual; the story/media exit gate stays open.
Strict coverage remains L2 75/120 and L3 41/90, and both games stay Preview.

## L3 introduction movie cue implementation

At exact implementation revision `8e9c06fb25b43824a8f66525276fa29317ea5380`
on 2 October, the native L3 original-movie gallery starts the verified
packaged DOS OPL2 Intro recording with `INTRO.FLI`. It pauses and resumes the
recording with the movie and stops it on dismissal or the final frame. Music
volume, global mute and the Silent music selection apply. Other movies have
no assigned cue until their original soundtrack is identified. The gallery
remains manual; this is not an automatic story transition.

`TEST_COMPILE_ONLY=1 TEST_SCOPE=dialogs zsh Scripts/run-app-integration-tests.sh`
passed (ignored log SHA-256
`9b72b950d1e9d49f2a2fcc666afb697a2d76affbb434d477640959b732714eb8`).
The focused dialog run passed the movie's start, pause, resume and close
checks with an injected audio probe. The wider dialog suite then failed on
“Tricky 1 did not match its checked hint data”; its ignored log SHA-256 was
`96ae95747e463701d1802a6df64bbcba4db7731a7b7ff795b58da244ed7c66bb`.
That later failure is outside this movie change, so the complete dialog suite
cannot be claimed as passing.

An isolated arm64 app built and passed `codesign --verify --deep --strict`.
Its executable SHA-256 was
`5731fe2052d6e1c025c102b810d27035fb3e0deb5a0da77bf808aae36bb54002`;
the bundled Intro AAC retained SHA-256
`1944653c46b6815c0b0f837c6175e0cd4307ca38a4489388eddea0e2a163b600`.
The ignored package log SHA-256 was
`64885d4ab6ece88b34983ec84c0604b1cddaf35579e29ea7dd8b553c198c62f1`.
The build reported stale Trolley certificates for this engine. Audible
output, frame synchronisation and controls on physical hardware remain
unverified. The introduction cue alone does not close story/media, campaign
or package exit gates. Strict coverage remains L2 75/120 and L3 41/90;
both games stay Preview.

## Engine-first route triage

At exact source revision `a20618bbb745d6abb0e4300d21689a755c958c6b`
on 2 October, the open-route evidence was reviewed against the original-game
comparisons and the tracked runtime. The original L3 Classic 3 walker clears
two eight-pixel rises without a tool, while the native four-pixel walker turns
at the first. Original Shadow 15 and Egyptian 26 frames also show unassisted
movement beyond rises that constrain the native routes. The existing
walker-only eight-pixel trial found a twice-replayed Classic 3 win, but
invalidated 24 of 41 recorded L3 routes. A terrain-bit filter still failed
19 routes. These results justify investigating collision sampling and step
timing in the L3 engine before more broad solver passes. They do not justify
changing the step limit globally.

For L2, the Space 02 guided native prefix reaches the upper route but has no
matched DOS PC action trace. The available published guides describe Amiga or
Super NES play. That case is a reason to compare magno-boot release,
platform placement and terrain contact with the supplied DOS engine. It is
not yet evidence of an L2 rule defect. The next engine batch should capture
matched original and native contact points for these levels, test one rule
at a time, then require every affected recorded fixture to pass twice before
promotion. No route, runtime, fixture, manifest or gate changed in this
triage. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both games stay Preview.

## Current-source L3 rise check and engine decision
