on 2 October, the current `NxlvKit` sources were rebuilt with Xcode's Swift
toolchain into a disposable library. An exact Classic 3 no-input trace found
walkers at `(143,80)` facing the first eight-pixel rise at native ticks 123,
146, 169 and 192. They carried bricks, a spade, no tool and no tool
respectively. The trace log SHA-256 is
`33d43cf36a42a1e8dbb312b6f89e725637bd8c9e5b8c40d09b0bdc9dd3e61a17`;

and `2f75357b784834e95647eaa5795d637c68a1dd34de044479166224c0ef0f9453`.

fixtures successfully; its log SHA-256 is
`9ac723fb187d0ae5fd34114833f85c9cd37f1693409b73b29fd8e94f89fa690a`.
The earlier unrestricted eight-pixel trial invalidated 24 of those 41 routes,

loss. L3 remains 41/90 and L2 remains 75/120; both stay Preview.

on 2 October, a disposable engine build raised only the walking lemming's

logged rise contacts from five to eight pixels. Classic 3's first eight-pixel
contact has terrain tag `0x0020` at x=144. Other recorded fixtures meet rises

SHA-256 was
`a71227d5dc266e1a9db06b09d53d1bc26af7b6a3adedf0fc4f527a1274685695`.

`c31f773ac6895c270191665208368dd951eb72a6d361c011ead48886c6f5d4cd`.

## L3 executable collision-rule inspection

At exact source revision `cd39a92050daaeb2328fb06aa8089fac8889f67f`
on 2 October, a temporary Capstone 5.0.9 environment inspected the supplied
DOS CD executable without changing or running it. `L3CD.EXE` SHA-256 was
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
Its MZ entry at file offset `0x2bbc0` begins with a relocation and
decompression stub, including a backwards byte copy and RLE branch. Direct
disassembly of those on-disk entry bytes therefore does not expose the
walking collision routine. This inspection yielded no source rule or engine
change. A memory image after unpacking, or a matched original gameplay
contact trace, is still needed before changing the tracked rise logic.
L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 DOS executable static unpack probe

At exact source revision `e052b8f117e9b3e8f001800d96f92775dfbd9664`
on 2 October, a temporary offline probe followed the supplied MZ entry
stub's backwards RLE and copy commands. Its 441 commands met at physical
offset `0x2096f`; the stub's saved target was `2f99:2db3` after adding the
chosen load segment. Capstone disassembly at that target yielded coherent
DOS startup instructions, including an `int 0x21` version call. This makes
an unpacked code image available for further static rule inspection without
launching a window. Segment relocations have not yet been applied, and this
is not a gameplay trace or a collision-rule finding.

The disposable probe, log and memory-image SHA-256 values were
`84f21ab5a01d9d15d26705bcd971747d8a8e1f91891efea64aaa92742b57e788`,

and `c979d4f6cddb8ccdf639fd69c18521a0da2556a524bdc889c74a11cc0d5fcf7e`.

tracked runtime, fixture or manifest changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3
remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original overlay movement structure

At exact source revision `a31986cc9b339a3054cf96568d7e531ba5a7276c`
on 2 October, offline inspection of the same supplied `L3CD.EXE` (SHA-256
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`)
found two further executable overlays at file offsets `0x2c600` and
`0x3f000`. Their code starts at `0x2e000` and `0x3fa00` respectively. The
second overlay contains the actor update path. At its code offset `0x7abf`,
an animation record adds horizontal and vertical deltas to actor movement
fields. The actor loop at `0x3cf4` then applies those fields to position.
The same overlay has an action-dependent contact handler at `0x7c0b`.
These offsets are structural evidence from static code inspection, not a
matched gameplay trace or a decoded walker rule. They show why changing the
native walker's maximum rise alone is an incomplete original-engine model.
The next step is to identify the ordinary walking animation and its contact
samples, then test a narrow native rule against both the original Classic 3
rise and all retained fixtures. No runtime, fixture or manifest changed;
L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 source movement-record correlation

At exact source revision `6088f192bf93c7ba4a13b37cb5e33f9d98d5b515`
on 2 October, an offline read of the supplied `GRAPHICS/LEMDATA3.DAT`
(SHA-256 `7b7f6cd447539797bc9b40aa3e61bdcf0c1060bb39d83ab477739380ce8550cf`)
correlated its first animation records with the original executable's actor
dispatcher. The executable's state table at second-overlay code offset
`0x73ea` sends states `0x1b` and `0x1c` to handlers at `0x5cf8` and
`0x5d69`. Those handlers select record offsets `0x0000`, `0x002a`,
`0x0054` and `0x007e` according to direction and alternating phase. Each
record has four frame entries; the first and third selected records carry
an eight-pixel horizontal anchor delta and mode `0`, while the alternate
records carry zero delta and mode `5`. The actor update at `0x7abf` consumes
both fields; the contact path at `0xc6f2` branches on those modes. The exact
state names and terrain-contact rule still require a matched trace, so this does
not establish a safe native timing or rise change. It does establish that
the original horizontal movement is phased by animation records rather
than a uniform one-pixel update. No engine, route or gate changed. L2
remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 cell-boundary contact prototypes

At exact source revision `ef23607628bb269b3ab4bdd480d27d10c232e577`
on 2 October, the movement records were compared with the second overlay's
actor loop. The loop reads queued frame offsets in reverse order and adds
them to the animation anchor. The frame offsets vary by one pixel, while
the anchor advances eight pixels at a record boundary. The contact path
at `0xc6f2` reads the anchor position. The visible foot position and the
ordinary walking contact point remain unverified; exact DOS contact timing
is still unmeasured.

Two disposable native builds tested that lead. The first checked ordinary
walking terrain only when the visible x position crossed an eight-pixel
cell boundary and allowed an eight-pixel rise there. It retained 12 of 41
fixtures. Its source, library and audit log SHA-256 values were

`3c6dcceadbf04012e23dbaf565edde642782434ceacad8d060d1be2416b354d9`,
`94e0f5c2203649bbfd8b51fb037b2d4c957836fab450e880f06f45d19b3031f4`

zero-reserve candidate at tick 970. Two further exact-level verifier runs
agreed, and the unchanged 273-entry manifest checked successfully. The
candidate retains fewer lemmings than the tracked Classic 3 fixture, so it
was not promoted. The solver log and candidate SHA-256 values were
`853f9f63f334650b1a0d9fd797d1de9a325ed63be0a87296e2c838cd0a114f41`

`973fb4edd55977ce4fa330f0c0bbbeb801e36dc4c27510c6211d699590ce5058`;
the log SHA-256 was

## L3 staged-rise prototype rejection and original rise records

At exact source revision `e9d7e644b9f8c1cbade9434919f58be8a71ad000`
on 2 October, a disposable native runtime spread rises above four pixels
over successive ticks at two vertical pixels per tick while horizontal
movement continued. Rises of up to four pixels retained the tracked rule.
The substituted source, compiled library, fixture-audit executable and
audit log SHA-256 values were
`36f19666d28f7ec2ffeaabad8fcc54a316037002172f539ce4436c46bf3f534a`,
`a3521b83632ded30faaf7d4b2e7a1028e6d310e898e3100de459bd37003e21af`,
`9f1924f841ad6a1fd2f566f3c662554937c2194b9c7a414337cbead0e1ecdbbc`
and `1ee02e23993e23a755d2eaa449ee7b2acd2ce97095b40e8123702c4fe90cfd56`.
Only 16 of 41 retained L3 fixtures passed; 25 failed. This prototype is
rejected. Do not repeat it on unchanged source and data.

An offline read of the supplied `GRAPHICS/LEMDATA3.DAT` (SHA-256
`7b7f6cd447539797bc9b40aa3e61bdcf0c1060bb39d83ab477739380ce8550cf`)
found four rise records for each direction and phase. The second-overlay
handler at code offset `0x5dc7` selects them when the actor's `+0x14`
field is 1, 2, 3 or 4. One right-facing set is at data offsets `0x174`,
`0x19e`, `0x1c8` and `0x1f2`; its record headers specify anchor deltas
`(+8,-2)`, `(+8,-4)`, `(+8,-6)` and `(+8,-8)`. The other right-facing
set starts at `0x224`; left-facing sets start at `0x2d4` and `0x384`
with matching negative horizontal deltas. The eight-pixel rise records
contain five frame entries; the others contain four. These are original
movement-data and dispatcher facts, not proof that the ordinary walker
uses every rise record. The setter for actor field `+0x14`, the terrain
probe and the exact contact tick still need a matched original-engine
trace before a tracked runtime rule can be changed. No engine, route,
manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and
both stay Preview.

## L3 original walker-to-rise control path

At exact source revision `87caf6073893eed4b92838e30d167184adf93d6a`
on 2 October, a further offline read of the same original `L3CD.EXE`
(SHA-256 `5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`)
traced a rise decision from terrain handling to the movement records.
The second-overlay routine at code offset `0xc91a` samples terrain near
the actor's animation anchor. At `0xcb97` it derives the actor's `+0x14`
field from one terrain result and selects action `8`. The action table at
`0x72b6` dispatches action `8` to `0x50c7`; that handler selects state
`9` when the current state is `0x1c`. The state table at `0x73ea` sends
state `9` to the rise-record selector at `0x5dc7`. Action `0` also selects
state `0x1c` for actor mode `0`, and that state's handler uses the phased
horizontal movement records. This establishes a connected original
walking-to-rise path; the earlier uncertainty about whether those records
can be reached from walking is resolved.

The terrain helper at `0xc7a6` counts up to four samples into `+0x14`,
but other terrain helpers also set that field. The exact sample geometry,
terrain bit meanings, actor anchor versus visible foot position, and tick
ordering still need a matched original-engine trace. The native runtime's
one-pixel walker cannot safely adopt the original eight-pixel animation
record delta without those details: three tested approximations invalidated
16, 29 or all 41 retained fixtures, and the staged-rise trial invalidated
25. No tracked engine, route, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains
75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original anchor and visible-frame timing

At exact source revision `73d35a584cfc08b15c861af9082f398ebfb6ab7b`
on 2 October, the original animation records and the second-overlay
frame queue were read together. The record loader at code offset `0x7abf`
adds its horizontal and vertical deltas to pending movement fields `+4`
and `+6`. The actor loop at `0x3cd4` applies those pending deltas to the
anchor fields `+8` and `+0xa` only when the queued frame count `+0xca`
reaches zero. The display update at `0x7266` then reads frame entries in
reverse order and adds their offsets to that anchor for the visible
position at fields `+0` and `+2`.

For the right-facing flat records at data offsets `0x0000` and `0x002a`,
the first record queues a later `+8` anchor delta and visible x offsets
`-2,-1,0,+1` in playback order. The second queues no anchor delta and
visible offsets `-6,-5,-4,-3`. Across the record boundary these produce
continuous one-pixel visible steps after the anchor advances by eight.
The rise records at `0x174` through `0x1f2` use phased x offsets
and add vertical frame offsets before their pending `-2` through `-8`
anchor deltas are applied. The eight-pixel rise takes five queued frames;
smaller rises take four. These values come from the supplied original
movement data, not a native gameplay trace.

The native one-pixel visible walking speed is therefore consistent with
these original records. The unresolved difference is the original
contact probe's anchor-relative position and frame phase, including
terrain-bit interpretation. Changing native visible speed or staging a
rise without that model has already broken retained routes. No engine,
route, fixture, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original rise-sampler bit classes

At exact tracked source revision
`34207aa0199a0cfe0d9e4b404aaf4d1e40c56f03` on 2 October,
an offline disassembly of the supplied DOS CD executable examined the
second overlay's rise path and terrain sampler. The executable SHA-256
was `5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
The temporary disassembly source and log SHA-256 values were
`3c19d30096564d6592a53b81fb057c752671e614a3321c0b325f7c92f7016453`
and `8f6e259e9c28240d2f10057f8c445a164511ab8b06afc814e1e29021f9207bb9`.

The terrain sampler at overlay offset `0x7597` takes four samples two
vertical pixels apart. It returns `0x20` for no selected bits, `0x40`
for a partial mask and `0x80` for all four samples. Its mode table
maps mode 4 to bit mask `0x10000000` and mode 0 to mask `0x00200088`.
In the rise path at `0xc9dd`, the engine samples eight anchor pixels
ahead and 14 pixels above the anchor with mode 4. It then samples the
same position with mode 0. Each branch requires result `0x20` before
the path continues. This supports a class-specific projected probe,
but the mapping from the native 16-bit scene tags to the original
32-bit cells and the actor-anchor alignment remain unverified.

A headless native contact probe found four `0x0060` projected tags at
the Classic `020` eight-pixel wall and four `0x1000` tags at the Classic
`016` six-pixel wall. The temporary probe source, executable and
two-contact log SHA-256 values were
`4860c3b262d1a487812385bf285b001a48e83c82dfb0451ef36d79b7e30e80f7`,
`828ff20896fc1f42a2d52c442bef7dc761982d08612788c647199b2b09e69e0e`
and `11597325bc9f216352e4cd599181fc3d5f6ee0faf05af6c34be23354361d86ba`.
The native `isSolid` check uses tag bit `0x20`, which does not model
both original sampler modes. No new rule passed original-engine parity
or the strict replay gate in this batch. No tracked engine, route,
fixture, manifest or gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original terrain-cell layout check

At exact tracked source revision
`75b477196f4770d9d1153e05bf300b227bdb772c` on 2 October,
an offline read of the relocated DOS CD main image and the supplied
second overlay traced the terrain lookup used by the rise sampler.
The main image SHA-256 was
`e83edd76861cadbed109960f0c7e1e88eb91e33ce666962a74ad33904e301a12`.
The temporary disassembly source and log SHA-256 values were
`f8ddd9a60d0c3b6f0c3601f3e70ac8db71e9e6c37db37544b7d752192dd0112b`
and `997d117d25ee47acc85bb792532bc065251021a63fa5e514cfb545e6d8eb4c38`.

The main lookup at offset `0x5b77` divides y by two and x by eight,
then multiplies the x column by four. It returns a pointer to a
32-bit terrain cell. In the second overlay's cell assembly at `0x264f`,
the engine reads a separate 32-bit source value, copies its `dh` byte
into `ah` and tests source bits. The branch at `0x26e0` sets or clears
cell bit `0x10000000` according to `ah & 4`, after tests of `ah & 1`
and existing cell bits `0x70`.

This establishes that the original mode-4 cell bit is assembled from
source fields and cell state. A direct shift from the native 16-bit
`0x1000` tag is not established. The rejected phase-0 class-probe
prototype used that unproved mapping. The source fields, native tag
mapping and actor anchor need further comparison before a safe rule
change. No tracked engine, route, fixture, manifest or gate changed.
L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 original terrain-operation queue and rise bit

At exact tracked source revision
`311a95868d967a1bd71c6adef24a2164b22f7763` on 3 October,
an offline 16-bit disassembly of the supplied DOS CD executable
traced the source of the terrain-cell bit tested by the original rise
sampler. `L3CD.EXE` SHA-256 remains
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`.
The second overlay's routine at code offset `0x2311` appends 12-byte
operation records. Its first dword comes from `edx`; the next two come
from `eax` and `ecx`. The terrain-cell assembly at `0x2625` processes
these records in reverse order. It copies bits 8–15 of the first dword
into `ah`, calls the terrain lookup, then sets or clears cell bit
`0x10000000`
according to `ah & 4` when `ah & 1` is clear and cell bits `0x70`
are clear.

The two local callers at `0x2730` and `0x2780` construct that high
byte from their arguments. The first starts with `dh = 2` and adds
mask `0x04` when its argument at `[bp+8]` is nonzero. The second starts
with `dh = 1` and adds mask `0x04` when its `[bp+8]` is nonzero, alongside
other operation flags. This establishes an operation-record path to
the original mode-4 cell bit. It does not establish which caller built
each cell in the two compared levels or a mapping from the supplied
16-bit object tags to the final 32-bit terrain grid. The lookup call's
effect on `ah` also remains untraced. In particular,
the rejected prototype's direct interpretation of native tag
`0x1000` remains unsupported.

The temporary 16-bit assembly and disassembly SHA-256 pairs were
`0229e7c16c87094152086904e1f62f3bcd6e2cc741c018d9d56226d568907603`
and `df54fe185fa5cf1a8e4d7cc14442b55f2822f8e90952a1340ca2baa43c76b21d`
for the cell loop, `998165ca7ec3a9766e724eedf7fbacf96a8acf0f421e2fdcef769e91fc6ae10b`
and `6dcf68a286a0b57d990281269336fa0f10f49ab5866e2333cefddc683e42f910`
for the record queue,
`d003d3a9beac967c82b0e5f83339675338a2f2be6df9705d763c3286729fcf37`
and `dd01704fd45057971b6c39f6e20d860360b7374c00f0543737635ea92b712ad1`
for the first caller, and
`0a22aa81b419ba727b72b63065065e672d3246747731e1791786dc158dcdb697`
and `cbd53ec1fab921eaac4dd841456674703fb7ddc7074ae315635b665d0b4eb361`
for the second caller. These files remain in `/tmp` and outside Git.
No native engine, route, fixture, manifest or exit gate changed. The
next comparison must connect original operation arguments to the
terrain cells at the Classic `020` and `016` contacts before changing
the rise rule. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

and `3795c57a76db04a38d1d2cf593bbdb160816bef586b3db20bf2dc505e05b5f52`.

Five retained routes still reached their original winning outcomes
under the prototype when replay inputs rejected by the changed state

## L3 Classic 015 prototype route and complete retained-route audit

At exact tracked source revision
`f2275029307995cc24aebd63eac925fe873c566c` on 3 October, an
offline, source-derived rise variant required the underlying temporary
object's uniform flags to equal `0x0020`. The contact in Classic `015`
comes from an object with uniform flags zero and an explicit `0x0020`
cell; this variant tested whether those sources should behave
differently. Its library SHA-256 was
`197a87aa66f3831e5e0ce40b62de8e10191c5139d59a5565ba879f58534fcf95`.
Only 36 of 41 temporary retained fixtures passed. Classic `015`,
Shadow `114`, and Egyptian `209`, `210` and `215` failed. The uniform
object flag does not isolate a valid original rule. This variant remains
disposable and must not be promoted or searched again on unchanged data.

A separate guided repair used the prior phase-zero `0x0020` prototype.
The tracked Classic `015` winner is lemming 1, saved at native tick 757.
Under the prototype, the first raised contact sends lemming 0 right;
this changes the terrain and displaces the eventual winner. Adding a
walker assignment for lemming 0 at tick 140 returned it towards the
lower route. Adding a walker assignment for lemming 1 at tick 600
restored the winner's direction before its existing tick-603 jump.
Moving its final jump from tick 722 to tick 723 produced the retained
outcome: 1 saved, 22 lost and none in reserve. All 37 inputs were
accepted. The exact level SHA-256 remained
`12fa2fee4174b01fd2de08aa19126cca5a85a0fbc0ebda216ba7dc5f99262e79`;
candidate-file SHA-256 was
`7a6b4f4a0ffc00cfacfa5afe43b3d168548c05874d6d97cf0065fc0427613cf5`.

The diagnostic replayed the candidate twice against the exact level
and initial-state identity. The independent completion verifier
replayed its temporary fixture twice. It then passed all 41 temporary
retained L3 fixtures in one batch; that log's SHA-256 was
`dcb9c743d1565a633f87c45c8487da8a8c0b6b99fad827b3978bc5be74c4efcb`.
The tracked 273-entry campaign manifest passed. The diagnostic source
and executable SHA-256 values were
`680b5792539667a7e19791f642d1f07f325528d9f1e21bb3cbd8d491aeed81a4`
and `e3918d18ffc18ef84bd2101b4d9d0f3e67265d43009358e9b427c9d85c32fe81`.
The `--require-all` verifier still reports 49 L3 levels without a
fixture, as expected; it is not a 90-level campaign pass.

This closes the **prototype's 41-route regression comparison**, not
the tracked engine's exit gate. The rise rule still needs original DOS
parity beyond the observed Classic `003` two-step path, followed by
production implementation and complete replay, campaign, media,
packaging and hardware evidence. No production engine, fixture,
manifest or exit gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90,
and both stay Preview.

## L3 phase-zero prototype impact on missing routes

At exact tracked source revision
`d3f310454a693255863bdec0683abd2c61d1feae` on 3 October, a
headless comparison ran each of the 49 L3 levels without a retained
fixture to tick 2,000 with no input. It used the tracked engine and the
disposable phase-zero `0x0020` prototype on the same supplied level
data. Five levels differed in lemming 0's checkpoint path or the
saved/lost/reserve result: Classic `008`, Shadow `117`, and Egyptian
`211`, `221` and `227`. At tick 2,000, Classic `008` had 0 lost and 10
in reserve under the tracked engine, against 20 lost and none in
reserve under the prototype. Egyptian `221` likewise changed from
0 lost and 10 in reserve to 20 lost and none in reserve. Egyptian
`227` changed from 0 lost and 10 in reserve to 18 lost and none in
reserve. These passive outcomes are not winning-route evidence, but
show that the new rule can change unverified campaign levels sharply.

## L3 Classic 29 rejected-action diagnosis and image-evidence correction

At exact tracked revision `34cf0369d178a8b50dd51de134600f9e4a367e4b`
on 4 October, a disposable headless probe replayed Classic `029`'s
tracked inputs under the direction-only prototype, skipping rejected
actions as in the route-repair trial. Immediately before input 14 at
tick 239, lemming 1 was at `(315, 338)`, walking left with no tool and
zero quantity. Its recorded `use upLeft` could not be accepted because
it had no bricks. The prototype starts both extra lemmings left:
`(24, 338)` and `(456, 336)`. The level has bricks at `(456, 306)`.
The changed direction plausibly prevents a pickup used by the old
route, but the probe does not establish the original game's initial
direction or a correct engine rule. The replay ended with 0 saved,
14 lost and 8 in reserve; 97 of its 389 inputs were rejected.

The earlier Shadow `115` image comparison overstated what an untimed
capture can prove. The first retained frame shows positions after an
unknown amount of simulation and possible turns. Its positions cannot
establish initial facing. The earlier sections now state this limit.
The direction-only prototype's 40 exact-replayed wins remain useful
diagnostic data, but they do not authorise a production direction
change. A timed original-engine trace is still needed before changing
that shared rule. A level-specific direction exception would have no
source basis.

The probe source, executable and log SHA-256 values were
`e930cf18d9410a9cf5e631f4d95025b0859eee1bc94750afe5917be081e1c2ba`,
`73dfa443cb80a69bf68f6bdfd6df22d9894e71c0b10d217aa4783996637c6dd9`
and `7e5867e8df6a9e12053baf327d52710a6c4bf9595f4a746d68e76ccecb6bc854`.

## L3 extra-object source-definition check

At exact tracked revision `7428c51a96e5ee578e1f7f1ea5082ccfeb7e7d0c`
on 4 October, a headless probe decoded the supplied permanent style
banks for all three tribes. Extra-object IDs `10006` and `10007` have
one 16-by-16 frame each in every bank. Their definition flags are
`0x04ff` and `0x04fe` respectively; the one-bit difference and distinct
frame pixels confirm that the supplied data distinguishes the object
types. They do not identify which ID starts left or right. The native
loader's current even-left/odd-right mapping is consistent with its
creature-ID convention, but this convention is not a DOS rule trace.
The untimed Shadow `115` capture also cannot settle that mapping.

The probe source, executable and log SHA-256 values were
`54526dcd9b26c7027c96a94077080c470bbfc7087fc2054765726f4a5ce2ee79`,

and `f56839b2b3e531a83fc59d667c8506c1fec04ca52253c89c1f53fede5b83cb7e`.
The repository has no script for dumping individual permanent object
definitions, so this used a direct temporary Swift build. It was
headless and muted. No engine, fixture, manifest or exit gate changed.
L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 extra-object pixel discriminator

At exact tracked revision `d85e801f1578e4c824b1328fd57d750643a65587`
on 4 October, the same decoded source frames were compared pixel by
pixel. IDs `10006` and `10007` differ at only 12 of 256 pixels, in
rows 3, 4, 6 and 7. In `10006`, the changed border-colour pixels lie
on the right at x=11–12; in `10007`, they lie on the left at x=3–4.
This is a directional visual cue in the supplied object art and gives
the reversed-extra prototype a stronger source-data lead than the
untimed DOSBox image. It remains an inference: the art does not prove
how the DOS code maps the object ID to an initial movement sign. The
prototype still lacks a Classic `029` win and original-engine timing.
No production direction rule is changed.

The source-frame probe and log hashes are recorded in the preceding
extra-object source-definition check. The pixel comparison used the
same decoded frames. Its temporary script and log SHA-256 values were

## Classic 28 original viewport alignment

At exact tracked revision `d70bb1f836f3b6faeb5d6fbcd4822dde2b4dc0a3`

An exact RGB comparison of the original 320-by-160 playfield against

all eight sampled gameplay frames. Pixel agreement ranged from 76.3%
to 77.0%; the next best offset, y 239, ranged from 46.4% to 46.9%.
Thus native `(160,368)` maps to visible playfield `(160,128)` in these
captures. The ignored alignment script, log, native scene and focused
contact montage are in the Classic `028` reference directory. The
archived script reproduced its output, and the refreshed `SHA256SUMS`
manifest passed.

This gives a coordinate basis for comparing the unassisted original
walkers on that stair with the native no-tool rise. It does not align
simulation ticks or prove the original 32-bit terrain-cell rule. The
current narrow correction remains supported; no broader engine rule,
winning fixture or exit gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains
41/90, and both stay Preview.

## Timed original Shadow 15 extra path and direction trial

At exact tracked revision `b0a6fcfe30f884ecceed10588df8ef6de0d7eeef`
on 4 October, a disposable original-game copy loaded supplied Shadow
`115` as its opening level. Source and copy SHA-256 values matched for
`LEVEL115.DAT` (`973fb4edd55977ce4fa330f0c0bbbeb801e36dc4c27510c6211d699590ce5058`),
`PERM115.OBS` (`9369a67124d437efc9608f1cbddd69d360a5ce910e1a317a0cb96d687387342e`)
and `TEMP115.OBS` (`b9c425e550418cba1e52c797aab2cf12d8238f7d2d70f9a7cd48b5bed0304e71`).
DOSBox Staging ran with dummy video and audio, software output and
`nosound=true`; the injector only opened the level. Its 320-by-200
gameplay AVI decoded 626 frames. The AVI SHA-256 is
`55fa65e40a9e99a6644366fa164ea049ddd6745c5f2b8c6aa057466a1f5f1708`.

The source-built native scene aligns best with the original at viewport
y 0 across sampled gameplay frames, at 53.3%-53.9% exact RGB agreement.
The lower extra starts at native `(256,84)`. A blue-sprite centroid
trace of original frames moves from about `(259,84)` at frame 7 to
`(259,88)` at frame 60, then `(269,77)` at frame 91. This shows rightward
movement and ascent over the stepped terrain without a player tool.
The source marker `10006` points right in the Shadow object art, while
`10007` points left. These images support a direction-mapping problem,
although sprite-centroid positions are not exact logic positions or
tick alignment. The original upper extra's initial direction remains
unresolved because it can turn before the later sampled movement.

The tracked native loader starts `10006` left. A disposable build
started only `10006` right. On Shadow `115`, its lower extra reached
`(263,96)` facing right by tick 15, then turned at x 264 rather than
rising eight pixels. That exact contact has immediate tag `0x0020`,
four projected `0x0020` samples and solid terrain from y 88 through
y 95. Thus changing direction alone does not reproduce the original
path; the rise rule also needs a grounded correction. The disposable
runtime-source and library SHA-256 values were
`b982303b9c818569e083478323124bfcfd633f0e06ec68812b30b03951fa8d9f`
and `671aa360839f76dff2a612798a928d9da3c9b157c36d77d33384357de60dbf53`.

The strict L3 gate passed the tracked 273-entry campaign manifest but
rejected a changed fixture identity, as expected for a changed initial
direction. A diagnostic outcome-only pass applied each retained input
sequence, skipped rejected actions, and twice exactly replayed any
temporary win. It retained wins on 39 of 41 levels; Classic `013`
saved 0 and lost 13, and Classic `015` saved 0 and lost 23. This
diagnostic script is direct Swift execution because the repository
gate correctly rejects altered identities and has no outcome-only
trial mode. Its outcome log SHA-256 is
`17e83d0643d001fe2cbff4ac94a83df8cae1ef450ba1e10cd5f3e4b59bda35cc`.
The ignored `.build/sequel-reference/l3-shadow115-timed-20261004/`
directory holds the AVI, decoded frames, centroid script, native
scene, source object images, trial source and logs under a checked
`SHA256SUMS` manifest.

The original path is new evidence for diagnosing extra direction and
walking rise. The trial loses retained wins, so neither rule is
promoted. No candidate, production engine, fixture or exit gate
changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay Preview.

## L3 extra direction correction and retained-route repair

From exact base revision `73130c8c4883727d7b32c6b2058ed61ad8d1a57a`

new state identities or removal of inputs rejected under the corrected
direction. In total, 17 L3 fixtures changed; none changed its
saved/lost/reserve count or ending tick.

The source-built strict L3 gate passed two exact-level replays of all
41 retained wins, the 273-entry campaign manifest and the missing
fixture, population, level hash, level number, outcome, rejected input

`054bcab7c36fb9612fb0f2568782aa50f6798e14ffcd94603b0905bf466008eb`.

A separate 45-second bounded solver pass on the corrected runtime
searched missing Shadow `115` with beam 128 and fewer inputs first.

and `73cd33c4cde2ea2dbfaf8f67112356655c414e6870c825ee444e8f995986f95b`.

The broad rule did not preserve retained outcomes. Classic `003`
fell from 10 saved to 7; Classic `015` and Egyptian `222` lost their

rise to contacts whose terrain samples 16 pixels ahead included an
opening. It preserved all 41 saved/lost/reserve counts using temporary
exact replays; Shadow `114` needed seven rejected inputs removed. The
separate strict gate passed those 41 temporary fixtures twice, the
unchanged 273-entry tracked manifest and its negative checks. The

`6123e67b4d81ef02541fd8d8b821925350c06994a8ea5c838e6ff6e684280512`
and `6a2add789dbd242a55b6f875ced3313088fd0e08971f43a5c383d91f53d8e683`.

shows an unassisted walker moving right and upward near the native
Classic `003` x 144 stair in frames 420–540. The narrow rule excludes

ahead remain solid. The capture lacks exact tick alignment, so it
does not prove the original terrain predicate, but it argues against

One bounded 45-second search of missing Shadow `115` on the narrow
model used initial beam 128 and fewer inputs first. It expanded

is `edc7d562b47c069fbda0b9823f490bdf4d9d0b0a02f007822fec1861c153a6a7`.

checked `SHA256SUMS` manifest.

Neither rise rule is promoted: the broad rule regresses retained
wins, and the narrow rule lacks convincing original-engine support.
No production source, tracked fixture, winning-route count or exit
gate changed. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90, and both stay

Shadow `115` remains without a winning route. No production engine,
tracked fixture, winning-route count or exit gate changed. L2 remains
75/120, L3 remains 41/90 and both remain Preview.

## L3 original Shadow 15 alignment and mixed plain-rise trial

At exact tracked revision `ba20470463f7fd2dc1c82b2153f49745ff240fd1`,
the existing headless original Shadow `115` capture was compared with
no-input native traces. The original lower extra's blue-sprite centroid
was sampled at frames 52–118. Fitting frame scale, frame offset and
sprite offsets on frames 52–85, then holding them fixed for frames
88–118, gave the broad and narrow plain-rise prototypes identical
held-out root-mean-square errors of 0.73 pixels in x and 1.62 in y.
The checked-in direction-only runtime gave 12.36 in x and 10.77 in y
under the same procedure. This supports an unassisted rise at the
lower extra's first `0x0020` step. It does not distinguish the broad
from the narrow rule or establish an exact original logic tick.

In the prior original Classic `003` no-input capture, a walker appears
near `(164,54)` in gameplay frame 540. On the broad prototype the
first extra reaches logic `(151,72)` and turns at x 152. That contact
has immediate native tag `0x0020` and projected tags
`[0x1000, 0x1000, 0x1000, 0x0020]`. A disposable variant allowing
phase-zero plain rises regardless of projected tags moved the same
actor through `(164,64)` at tick 144; the sprite's vertical offset is
consistent with the original image. This is spatial support for a
second rise, but the capture does not identify the actor or exact
tick well enough to prove the general terrain predicate.

Two disposable runtime rules were audited against all 41 retained L3
input sequences: any phase-zero `0x0020` contact, and a right-facing
rule admitting either four projected plain tags or the observed
three-`0x1000`, one-`0x0020` pattern. Each left 37 levels with a
twice-replayed exact win after rejected inputs were removed, but each
lost the retained wins on Classic `003`, Classic `015`, Shadow `114`
and Egyptian `222`. The mixed rule's extra contacts on Classic `015`
at `(240,268)` and `(256,378)` and Shadow `114` at `(168,74)` and
`(216,172)` share the observed projected-tag pattern. The trial
does not identify an original rule that separates these contacts.
Neither rule is promoted. The temporary runtime source SHA-256 values
are `1d8cad8afea775753389629a15eb74702fc3b539f0c8c329f26ccbae980e7b6e`
and `dab2b17122f0d0a61a234d00e6b741336c9097bfb9960fb44ad73354684b5a14`;
their library values are
`01d77690d246a620383458ac18cde3e02bf26023aa58f07958ebc60b35234453`
and `5deeafb5c8f459651e984ed20f7f39d8bd32b18c20909833805ad11c9f2dfa0f`.
The ignored `.build/sequel-reference/l3-plain-mixed-rise-20261004/`
directory contains both full outcome logs, paired traces, alignment
script and its checked `SHA256SUMS` file, SHA-256
`8205a02988652f523a8aa8d10be390fca34af7e1047541f4de401c178e362406`.
The tracked 273-entry campaign manifest still passed. Direct Swift
compilation was necessary to link the disposable runtime rules;
repository scripts build the checked-in engine.

No production engine, tracked fixture, winning-route count or exit
gate changed. The original plain-rise predicate, four retained-route
regressions, other missing routes and full campaign and package gates
remain open. L2 remains 75/120, L3 remains 41/90; both remain Preview.

### 4 October disputed attribute-grid shape check

At exact tracked revision `b53d631f98306e076b7649bd0cf4e5ecdaa7940f`,

directly. Classic `015` object `118`, Shadow `114` object `421` and

16-bit tag per cell. Object `118` is a two-by-eight wedge whose upper
four rows have only the right `0x0020` cell. Object `315` is a
two-by-four wedge whose lower two rows have only the left `0x0020`
cell. Object `421` is a five-by-twelve stepped grid. Classic `003`
object `48` and Shadow `114` object `486` instead use uniform object
flags with no grid. The ignored evidence pack above now includes the
attribute-grid diagnostic log, whose SHA-256 is

Shadow `115` rise from the Classic `015` retained-route regression.

No production change, new route or gate resulted. L2 remains 75/120,
L3 remains 41/90 and both remain Preview.

walking in the baseline, but was still building in the eight-use
trial. The recorded Walker at that tick reversed the baseline
lemming but only stopped the trial builder. A bounded pass tried an
extra Walker at ticks 555 through 560. Only a second Walker in the
same tick 560 retained the rescue; moving the recorded Walker to
ticks 561 through 563 did not. The disposable same-tick candidate
passed two exact simulation replays with 1 saved, 19 lost and 0
reserves at tick 1560, plus the unchanged tracked 273-entry campaign
manifest check. Its SHA-256 is
`b2fdac147b1eb9b14e79b7fc151aa48109a70980b0c3b7ef0079955a84c0db20`
at `/private/tmp/l3-brick-audit-20261004/repaired/108.json`.
The original DOS CD input sequence has not been matched, so this is
diagnostic only.

A separate disposable engine hypothesis made one Walker assignment
reverse an active builder. It restored Shadow 08's retained result
without a second input, but the full 41-route impact probe retained
only 33 routes and regressed Classic 05 and 21 and Shadow 01, among
other unresolved routes. The rule is not supported as a safe general
correction. Trial library SHA-256 is
`97d3db65b661bcd73638cf25c705aef6c4ce61f246c731c6789dc1b6694e28fe`;
the audit log SHA-256 is
`f657518e79dc21b569dccaa770bc85bfbbc4710a82da84607e3dd6eb12d57dc4`.
Both are in `/private/tmp/l3-brick-turn-builder-20261004`.
No engine rule, fixture, route count or exit gate changed; L2 remains
75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 4 October original Brick inventory correction and retained L3 replay gate

Starting from exact tracked revision `f993436bd6ba4560613730a5396bbafed133f0be`, the original DOS CD Classic tutorial's Brick pickup shows eight uses in the captured HUD. The source level SHA-256 is `2e62000858df2628606bbc99713bfe3e61fb2cf2afb80d221921e2bd9ff7b6cb`; the muted, headless DOSBox capture is `/private/tmp/l3-brick-hover-mid-20261004/captures/video0004.avi` (SHA-256 `2a0b2d209577547e202e2dd9af8650930ded6e0bc276c47c89f596688a54c2b2`), with frame 880 SHA-256 `30aa41e5697dcbfd8a06bdb54b93f631fe97e82870209dc648a3e900d77934e7`. The native L3 Brick pickup now supplies eight uses. Spade remains six and Grenade four. The runtime quantity assertions were updated accordingly.

The disposable eight-use trial initially retained only 8 of the 41 recorded L3 winning inputs unchanged. Bounded, level-specific repairs produced a candidate for every retained route; 33 tracked fixtures changed (25 for result identity alone and eight for input or outcome changes). Each candidate passed two exact-level replays with its source hash and saved/reserve result checked before promotion. The complete tracked `Scripts/run-lemmings3-runtime-tests.sh` suite passed headlessly with `LEMMINGS_TEST_AUDIO=muted`. `Tools/CampaignCompletion/report.py` regenerated a 273-entry manifest, and the tracked `Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh` gate passed the manifest, all 41 distinct exact-level replays, and its missing fixture, population, hash, level number, outcome, rejected input and ordering negative checks. The gate still reports 49 L3 levels without a verified fixture. L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90; both remain Preview.

This establishes retention of the native winning fixtures under the corrected Brick quantity. Egyptian 02 and Shadow 08 currently need two Walker assignments to the same actor in one simulation tick. That is accepted by the native replay interface, but feasibility through the original DOS CD player input remains unconfirmed. Original-engine route parity, continuous campaign and ending progression, recovery and result identity, media and story, packaged app and hardware evidence remain open. Do not treat this local replay gate as the 1.8 exit gate.

### 5 October same-tick Walker route input audit

At exact source revision `6e35a35a3cdea6939dc47b23f5c1d8564766d71a`, the native input path was inspected for the two same-tick Walker assignments in the retained Shadow 08 and Egyptian 02 fixtures. `Lemmings3PlayWindow.applyAction` applies an accepted assignment immediately and records its current `game.tick`; it has no one-assignment-per-tick guard. The keyboard and pointer paths can call it more than once before `advanceTick`, and the simulation clock is 23 ticks per second. `Lemmings3Runtime.assign` accepts Walker on a builder to stop building, then accepts Walker again on the resulting walker to turn it. This establishes that the two fixture actions match a possible native input sequence. It does not prove that a human can reliably issue both within one tick or that the original DOS CD accepts the same sequence. No code, fixture or gate changed. Original input parity and the remaining 1.8 exit gates stay open; L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October paired-Walker solver branch and bounded check

Starting from exact tracked revision `84b2d1fbbe11f767f5411c12f0be2268c95e1c58`, the L3 search was found to offer at most one assignment at each decision point even though the retained Shadow 08 and Egyptian 02 native routes require two Walker assignments on the same builder in one tick. It remains a solver branch; the game engine and fixture semantics are unchanged. It covered 905 passive decision points at depth 945, reached beam 256 across two rounds, expanded 1,164,063 states and found no win; the best partial saved 0 and lost 0. The candidate directory `/private/tmp/l3-paired-walker-classic20-20261005` is empty. The solver executable SHA-256 is `834e46b882df11eb03f37f35f610c3338de8ce772393d7729222b33176b07519` and its source-built library SHA-256 is `ca77cd87c28204e0893200b54b64563cb55bb86f487dd5dc25a37a977d243014`. Do not repeat this bound on unchanged source and level data. No new winning fixture or 1.8 exit gate closed. L2 remains 75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October Classic 15 control-source check

right edge and describes Walker turning a walking lemming. It does
not describe a vertical scroll control. This text reports Amiga use;
it is not a DOS CD control specification. The original DOS manual scan
at `https://db.hfsplay.fr/files/2018/10/06/c9f24f28-42dd-4046-aa9b-1d0b7622142e.pdf`

engine rule or route was promoted from this source check. The second
Classic 15 Walker and disputed rise remain unmatched; L2 remains
75/120 and L3 41/90, both Preview.

### 5 October original ending threshold

The supplied DOS `L3CD.EXE` (SHA-256
`5eef913b578fb642eef183d80404d91c4cbcb1f4c998e184962ce1f37f64f018`)
was unpacked from Microsoft EXEPACK for a bounded control-flow check. The
unpacked image SHA-256 is
`3bef59cedb516be505edaa42084fc9b136575f2964b7396cc5e3dc6de0544c04`.
The result writer at overlay offsets `0x38dc4`–`0x38e55` maps levels 1–30,
101–130 and 201–230 into three 31-byte groups. Their final-level result
slots are `0x1e`, `0x3d` and `0x5c`. At `0x2e957`, the game requires the
current tribe's final lemming count to be at least `0x32` (50) before marking
that tribe complete. At `0x2ed22`, it checks the other two final-level slots
against 50 before calling `THE-END.FLI`. The nearby original result strings
also state that 50 lemmings are required to complete a tribe.

The native flow previously treated 90 recorded level wins as full title
completion, irrespective of the three final survivor counts, and did not
automatically play `THE-END.FLI`. The original result threshold is now an
explicit 1.8 gate. The bounded binary check did not prove bit-for-bit DOS
carry arithmetic, so native survivor-count equivalence still needs campaign
traces.

### 5 October native finale flow

Native player progress now marks a tribe complete when its recorded final
result has at least 50 survivors. This follows the observed DOS ending
check even when an earlier level was skipped. The strict 1.8 replay gate
separately requires all 30 levels in each continuous tribe chain.
`THE-END.FLI` opens only after the result that first completes the third
eligible tribe. Finishing or skipping it returns to
the library or standalone game menu; missing or unreadable automatic media
also completes the handover. The pending ending is consumed before playback,
so a repeated Continue action cannot queue it again. An under-50 finale shows
the target and offers Choose level.

The focused runtime tests passed the 49/50 boundary and skipped-level finale
cases.
The offscreen, muted `l3-story` app test passed the finale decision cases,
movie handovers, rendered under-50 state and accessibility action target. The
normal L3 chain verifier still passed 15 carried results with three open
chains; its strict mode failed as required at Classic 07, Shadow 106 and
Egyptian 205. This validates the source-level result flow, not a played
three-tribe campaign, exact DOS carry arithmetic, movie soundtrack or
packaged player journey. L3 remains Preview.

### L3 survivor budget from the supplied level headers

Each tribe starts with 20 lemmings. The 30 exact `LEVEL*.DAT` headers add 39
Classic, 36 Shadow and 49 Egyptian lemmings in total. A 50-survivor finish
therefore allows at most 9 losses in Classic, 6 in Shadow and 19 in Egyptian
across the full tribe. These are upper bounds even if every remaining level
has a perfect route. A winning fixture or an extended campaign prefix does
not close the ending gate when its cumulative losses exceed this budget.

the focused, headless, muted runtime suite passed the constructed-brick
follower-rise test. Nine changed standalone routes were repaired and each
double-replayed. Classic 06 now saves 10 with no loss at both its standalone
population and the carried population of 13. Shadow 103 now does the same at
standalone population 20 and carried population 15. Constructed-brick contact
still needs an exact original DOS comparison.

The normal `Scripts/verify-l3-completion.sh` gate passed its 282-entry
manifest, all 50 recorded standalone wins, malformed-evidence tests and the
recorded campaign prefixes. The manifest SHA-256 is
`fa111bf5b763992532f7bd88279d418cf204f88b4e2c17017c58e6ef91faf834`.
The campaign record SHA-256 is
`32bca9b2454cb5c2b95380a866507170fa1c2eb461fa4838fa011128059df016`:
19 linked results, then gaps at Classic 09 with one lemming, Shadow 105 with
two and Egyptian 208 with five. Forty standalone wins are still missing.
The current linked Classic and Shadow prefixes already exceed their full
50-survivor loss budgets; Egyptian has used its full allowance.

The campaign verifier now double-replays every retained carried-population
fixture, including routes after a current chain gap. This exposed stale
Shadow 107 and 108 one-lemming routes. Both were repaired with accepted
inputs and the headless, muted campaign check passed all 26 retained carried
fixtures. This check verifies individual carried starts; it does not turn
them into a continuous campaign result.

The headless cross-build recovery suite passed on recovery source SHA-256
`3318656a5adbbdbf19d65e83f73948b37a2917d62d6da94460c7e27b1c75041d`.
It now restores a saved L3 run made with six-use Spade pickups, including
one with the older tool mapping, only when the old initial and replay state
hashes match. The offscreen, muted `l3-story` integration test passed opening,
ending, controls and handovers. The rebuilt arm64 standalone L3 Preview app
passed `codesign --verify --deep --strict`; its executable SHA-256 is
`ec44e9e3d11a209698fe3601991be1fca385e5091af4fe9480335d77cb5ea15a`.
This app is a source check, not a 1.8 release candidate or proof of a
packaged player journey. All L3 release gates remain open and L3 stays Preview.

### 5 October lossless Egyptian 08 carried route

The exact Egyptian 208 route at carried population five saves all five with
no losses or reserve at tick 4,830. All 34 inputs were accepted in two strict
headless replays. Its fixture SHA-256 is
`c57196296883984f07e8f985aba72469833ce6bde64a465c5244bb70beee8b7d`.
The refreshed chain record SHA-256 is
`3b0a6e171413e23423a26e962e6ad7f7fce79bf97892a4ab8090c92b4cc61574`:

Egyptian chain now stops at level 209 with five lemmings. It has still used
all 19 losses allowed for a 50-survivor ending, so every remaining Egyptian
level needs a lossless route unless an earlier result improves.

### 5 October self-contained local bundle and solver check

The attached worktree links ignored source game assets to the primary
checkout. The full app build previously copied those links into `Ports`, so
the bundled Classic data was absent from the session test. The resource copy
now follows the source links. The rebuilt arm64 app has no links under its
bundled `Ports` directory and passes `codesign --verify --deep --strict`.
Its executable SHA-256 is
`b287ae1dad62c12dcbf6f5533df978d49cbb1762ae70a08f57d4f1768b581250`.
The offscreen, muted `sessions` checks pass saved Solo and Hot Seat flows
across Classic, L2 and L3. The `l3-story` checks pass opening and ending
policies, controls, handovers and optional-media failure paths against this
full local bundle. The build is ad hoc and is not the 1.8 release candidate;
packaged gameplay and recovery journeys remain unverified.

The L3 solver now counts a live Blocker as a potential survivor because
Walker can release it. A stale Classic 06 seed test now uses the retained
carried fixture. A shorter search horizon accepts later donor inputs because
it applies only the prefix up to the chosen checkpoint. The focused,
headless, muted `Scripts/run-lemmings3-solver-tests.sh` passed. This fixes
search ranking and seed validation; it does not establish a winning route.
